<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q001"></a>
## Q1. Private member's bills in Parliament
**Primary route:** Polity → [Parliament](../../topics/polity/parliament.md) → Private member's bills in Parliament  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Parliament → Private member's bills in Parliament  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q1 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Parliament.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Parliament.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-private-member-s-bills-in-parliament`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to the Parliament of India, consider the following statements:
1. A private member's bill is a bill presented by a Member of Parliament who is not elected but only nominated by the President of India.
2. Recently, a private member's bill has been passed in the Parliament of India for the first time in its history.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Neither 1 nor 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect: A 'Private Member' is defined as any Member of Parliament (MP) who is not a Minister in the Union Council of Ministers. This includes both elected and nominated members of either the Lok Sabha or the Rajya Sabha. Therefore, the statement that it is only presented by nominated members is false (Source: M. Laxmikanth, Indian Polity, Chapter on Parliament; Lok Sabha Rules of Procedure). 

Statement 2 is incorrect: Private Member's Bills have been passed in the Parliament of India multiple times. According to official records and research by PRS Legislative Research, a total of 14 Private Member's Bills have become law in the history of the Indian Parliament. The last such bill to be passed by both Houses and receive Presidential assent was 'The Supreme Court (Enlargement of Criminal Appellate Jurisdiction) Bill, 1968', which became an Act in 1970. Since then, no private member's bill has been passed by both Houses (Source: PRS Legislative Research; Rajya Sabha Secretariat).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **private member's bills in Parliament**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q002"></a>
## Q2. Rigvedic Aryans and Indus Valley cultural differences
**Primary route:** Ancient History → [Rig Vedic Age](../../topics/ancient-history/rig-vedic-age.md) → Rigvedic Aryans and Indus Valley cultural differences  
**Primary classification:** Ancient History → Rig Vedic Age → Rigvedic Aryans and Indus Valley cultural differences  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q2 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Ancient-Indian-History/basic/08_Rig-Vedic-Age.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Ancient-Indian-History/basic/08_Rig-Vedic-Age.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `ancient-history-rigvedic-aryans-and-indus-valley-cultural-differences`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to the difference between the culture of Rigvedic Aryans and Indus Valley people, which of the following statements is/are correct?
1. Rigvedic Aryans used the coat of mail and helmet in warfare whereas the people of Indus Valley Civilization did not leave any evidence of using them.
2. Rigvedic Aryans knew gold, silver and copper whereas Indus Valley people knew only copper and iron.
3. Rigvedic Aryans had domesticated the horse whereas there is no evidence of Indus Valley people having been aware of this animal.

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The question compares the cultures of the Rigvedic Aryans and the Indus Valley Civilization (IVC) based on three parameters: warfare, metallurgy, and domestication of animals.

1. **Statement 1 is correct**: Rigvedic Aryans were a martial society and the Rigveda mentions protective gear like the 'Varman' (coat of mail) and 'Sipra' (helmet). In contrast, the Indus Valley Civilization was primarily a peaceful urban culture; archaeological excavations have yielded weapons like daggers and spears, but no evidence of defensive armor like helmets or coats of mail has been found (NCERT Class 12, Themes in Indian History Part 1).

2. **Statement 2 is incorrect**: While Rigvedic Aryans were familiar with gold, silver, and copper (referred to as 'Ayas'), the Indus Valley people were also familiar with gold, silver, and copper/bronze. However, the Indus Valley people did **not** know iron. Iron was discovered and used much later, during the Later Vedic period (around 1000 BCE). The statement's claim that IVC people knew 'only copper and iron' is doubly incorrect as they knew other metals and lacked iron (RS Sharma, Ancient India).

3. **Statement 3 is correct**: The horse was central to Rigvedic culture, used for chariots and mentioned extensively in the Vedas. Regarding the IVC, while some controversial remains (like those at Surkotada) have been identified as horse bones, there is no clear evidence of the horse being a domesticated or culturally significant animal in the Indus Valley. UPSC's official answer key for the 2017 Prelims accepts this statement as correct, emphasizing the lack of substantial evidence for horse awareness/domestication in IVC compared to the Aryans (UPSC Official Answer Key 2017).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **rigvedic Aryans and Indus Valley cultural differences**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q003"></a>
## Q3. Recognition of Prior Learning Scheme
**Primary route:** Economy → [Employment, Labour Codes, Skills and Demographic Dividend](../../topics/economy/employment-labour-codes-skills-and-demographic-dividend.md) → Recognition of Prior Learning Scheme  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Employment, Labour Codes, Skills and Demographic Dividend → Recognition of Prior Learning Scheme  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q3 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/22_Employment-Labour-Codes-Skills-and-Demographic-Dividend.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/22_Employment-Labour-Codes-Skills-and-Demographic-Dividend.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-recognition-of-prior-learning-scheme`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

'Recognition of Prior Learning Scheme' is sometimes mentioned in the news with reference to

(a) Certifying the skills acquired by construction workers through traditional channels.  
(b) Enrolling the persons in Universities for distance learning programmes.  
(c) Reserving some skilled jobs to rural and urban poor in some public sector undertakings.  
(d) Certifying the skills acquired by trainees under the National Skill Development Programme.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — Certifying the skills acquired by construction workers through traditional channels.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The 'Recognition of Prior Learning (RPL)' scheme is a key component of the Pradhan Mantri Kaushal Vikas Yojana (PMKVY), launched by the Ministry of Skill Development and Entrepreneurship (MSDE). Its primary objective is to provide formal certification to the large uncertified workforce in India who have acquired skills through informal or traditional channels, such as construction workers, artisans, and laborers in unregulated sectors. By assessing and certifying these existing skills, the scheme aligns them with the National Skills Qualifications Framework (NSQF), thereby enhancing the employability, mobility, and social inclusion of the workforce. Unlike standard training programs that focus on fresh skilling, RPL specifically targets individuals with prior experience who lack formal documentation of their expertise. Certified candidates often receive benefits like a cash reward (e.g., ₹500), accidental insurance, and exposure to digital and financial literacy.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Certifying the skills acquired by construction workers through traditional channels.** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: Enrolling the persons in Universities for distance learning programmes.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Reserving some skilled jobs to rural and urban poor in some public sector undertakings.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Certifying the skills acquired by trainees under the National Skill Development Programme.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **recognition of Prior Learning Scheme**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q004"></a>
## Q4. Sathyamangalam as Eastern-Western Ghats ecological link
**Primary route:** Environment and Ecology → [Protected Area Network in India](../../topics/environment/protected-area-network-india.md) → Sathyamangalam as Eastern-Western Ghats ecological link  
**Primary classification:** Environment and Ecology → Protected Area Network in India → Sathyamangalam as Eastern-Western Ghats ecological link  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q4 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/06_Protected-Area-Network-India.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/06_Protected-Area-Network-India.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-and-ecology-sathyamangalam-as-eastern-western-ghats-ecological-link`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

From the ecological point of view, which one of the following assumes importance in being a good link between the Eastern Ghats and the Western Ghats?

(a) Sathyamangalam Tiger Reserve  
(b) Nallamala Forest  
(c) Nagarhole National Park  
(d) Seshachalam Biosphere Reserve  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — Sathyamangalam Tiger Reserve**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Sathyamangalam Tiger Reserve (STR), located in the Erode district of Tamil Nadu, is the correct answer because it is situated at the confluence of the Western Ghats and the Eastern Ghats. Ecologically, it serves as a vital wildlife corridor and a genetic link between these two mountain ranges, facilitating the movement of species like tigers and elephants across the Nilgiri Biosphere Reserve and the Eastern Ghats landscape. It adjoins other protected areas such as the Billigiriranga Swamy Temple (BRT) Wildlife Sanctuary, Sigur Plateau, and Mudumalai National Park. In recognition of its conservation efforts, STR was awarded the prestigious TX2 Award in 2022 for doubling its tiger population since 2010. In contrast, the Nallamala Forest and Seshachalam Biosphere Reserve are primarily located within the Eastern Ghats (Andhra Pradesh/Telangana), while Nagarhole National Park is situated in the Western Ghats (Karnataka).

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Sathyamangalam Tiger Reserve** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: Nallamala Forest** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Nagarhole National Park** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Seshachalam Biosphere Reserve** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **sathyamangalam as Eastern-Western Ghats ecological link**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q005"></a>
## Q5. Equality and absence of privileges
**Primary route:** Polity → [Fundamental Rights](../../topics/polity/fundamental-rights.md) → Equality and absence of privileges  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Fundamental Rights → Equality and absence of privileges  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q5 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Fundamental-Rights.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Fundamental-Rights.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-equality-and-absence-of-privileges`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

One of the implications of equality in society is the absence of

(a) Privileges  
(b) Restraints  
(c) Competition  
(d) Ideology  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — Privileges**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Equality in a political and social context primarily implies the absence of special privileges for any specific section of society. According to NCERT Class 11 Political Theory (Chapter 3: Equality), the first step towards achieving equality is ending formal systems of inequality and privileges. Similarly, M. Laxmikanth's 'Indian Polity' (Chapter 4: Preamble of the Constitution) defines equality as the 'absence of special privileges to any section of the society, and the provision of adequate opportunities for all individuals without any discrimination.' While the 'absence of restraints' (Option 1) is a definition of Liberty, equality focuses on ensuring that no individual is given preferential treatment over others based on birth, caste, or creed. Competition (Option 2) is not absent in an equal society; rather, equality of opportunity ensures that competition is fair. Ideology (Option 3) is irrelevant as equality itself is a core political ideology.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Privileges** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: Restraints** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Competition** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Ideology** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **equality and absence of privileges**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q006"></a>
## Q6. TRAFFIC wildlife-trade monitoring organisation
**Primary route:** Environment and Ecology → [CITES and Wildlife Trade](../../topics/environment/multilateral-environmental-conventions-cbd-basel-stockholm-montreal.md) → TRAFFIC wildlife-trade monitoring organisation  
**Primary classification:** Environment and Ecology → CITES and Wildlife Trade → TRAFFIC wildlife-trade monitoring organisation  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q6 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/09_CITES-and-Wildlife-Trade.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/09_CITES-and-Wildlife-Trade.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-and-ecology-traffic-wildlife-trade-monitoring-organisation`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements in respect of Trade Related Analysis of Fauna and Flora in Commerce (TRAFFIC):
1. TRAFFIC is a bureau under United Nations Environment Programme (UNEP).
2. The mission of TRAFFIC is to ensure that trade in wild plants and animals is not a threat to the conservation of nature.

Which of the above statements is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect: TRAFFIC (Trade Related Analysis of Fauna and Flora in Commerce) is not a bureau under the United Nations Environment Programme (UNEP). It is a leading non-governmental organization (NGO) and was established in 1976 as a joint program of the World Wildlife Fund (WWF) and the International Union for Conservation of Nature (IUCN). It operates as a strategic alliance between these two organizations to monitor wildlife trade. 

Statement 2 is correct: The official mission of TRAFFIC is 'to ensure that trade in wild plants and animals is not a threat to the conservation of nature.' It works globally on trade in wild animals and plants in the context of both biodiversity conservation and sustainable development, aiming to ensure that wildlife trade is legal and sustainable.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **tRAFFIC wildlife-trade monitoring organisation**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q007"></a>
## Q7. DPSP addition by the Forty-second Amendment
**Primary route:** Polity → [Directive Principles of State Policy](../../topics/polity/directive-principles.md) → DPSP addition by the Forty-second Amendment  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Directive Principles of State Policy → DPSP addition by the Forty-second Amendment  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q7 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Directive-Principles.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Directive-Principles.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-dpsp-addition-by-the-forty-second-amendment`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which principle among the following was added to the Directive Principles of State Policy by the 42nd Amendment to the Constitution?

(a) Equal pay for equal work for both men and women  
(b) Participation of workers in the management of industries  
(c) Right to work, education and public assistance  
(d) Securing living wage and human conditions of work to workers  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Participation of workers in the management of industries**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The 42nd Amendment Act of 1976, often referred to as the 'Mini-Constitution', added four new Directive Principles to the Indian Constitution: 
1. Article 39: To secure opportunities for the healthy development of children.
2. Article 39A: To promote equal justice and to provide free legal aid to the poor.
3. Article 43A: To take steps to secure the participation of workers in the management of industries.
4. Article 48A: To protect and improve the environment and to safeguard forests and wildlife.

Among the given options:
- Option 0 (Equal pay for equal work) is part of the original Article 39(d).
- Option 1 (Participation of workers in management) was added by the 42nd Amendment as Article 43A.
- Option 2 (Right to work, education, and public assistance) is part of the original Article 41.
- Option 3 (Securing living wage and human conditions of work) is part of the original Article 43.

References: M. Laxmikanth, Indian Polity, Chapter on Directive Principles of State Policy; NCERT Class 11 Political Science - Indian Constitution at Work; Constitution of India, Article 43A.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Equal pay for equal work for both men and women** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: Participation of workers in the management of industries** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: Right to work, education and public assistance** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Securing living wage and human conditions of work to workers** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **dPSP addition by the Forty-second Amendment**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q008"></a>
## Q8. Rights and duties under law
**Primary route:** Polity → [Fundamental Rights](../../topics/polity/fundamental-rights.md) → Rights and duties under law  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Fundamental Rights → Rights and duties under law  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q8 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Fundamental-Rights.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Fundamental-Rights.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-rights-and-duties-under-law`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following statements is correct?

(a) Rights are claims of the State against the citizens.  
(b) Rights are privileges which are incorporated in the Constitution of a State.  
(c) Rights are claims of the citizens against the State.  
(d) Rights are privileges of a few citizens against the many.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Rights are claims of the citizens against the State.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

In political theory and constitutional law, rights are understood as justified claims of individuals that are necessary for their development and for leading a life of dignity. According to NCERT Class 11 Political Theory (Chapter 5), rights are 'primarily those claims that I along with others regard to be necessary for leading a life of respect and dignity.' They represent a set of entitlements that the state is obligated to recognize and protect. Furthermore, as explained in M. Laxmikanth's 'Indian Polity' (Chapter 7), Fundamental Rights in the Indian Constitution are intended to protect the liberties and freedoms of the people against the 'invasion by the State.' They operate as limitations on the tyranny of the executive and arbitrary laws of the legislature, effectively making them claims of the citizens against the State. Rights are distinct from 'privileges' (Options 1 and 3) because privileges are special advantages granted to specific individuals or groups, whereas rights are universal and fundamental entitlements.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Rights are claims of the State against the citizens.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: Rights are privileges which are incorporated in the Constitution of a State.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Rights are claims of the citizens against the State.** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: Rights are privileges of a few citizens against the many.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **rights and duties under law**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

## Q9. Global Gender Gap Index publisher
**Primary route:** Social Justice → [Women and Gender Justice](../../topics/social-justice/women-and-gender-justice.md) → Global Gender Gap Index publisher  
**Primary classification:** Social Justice → Women and Gender Justice → Global Gender Gap Index publisher

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following gives 'Global Gender Gap Index' ranking to the countries of the world?

(a) World Economic Forum  
(b) UN Human Rights Council  
(c) UN Women  
(d) World Health Organization  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — World Economic Forum**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Global Gender Gap Index is published annually by the World Economic Forum (WEF). First introduced in 2006, the index benchmarks the evolution of gender-based gaps across four key dimensions: Economic Participation and Opportunity, Educational Attainment, Health and Survival, and Political Empowerment (Source: World Economic Forum, Global Gender Gap Report). The index provides a framework for capturing the magnitude of gender-based disparities and tracking their progress over time. According to the Global Gender Gap Report 2024, the global gender gap score in 2024 stands at 68.5%, indicating that it will take approximately 134 years to reach full parity at the current rate of progress (Source: WEF). While organizations like UN Women and the UN Human Rights Council work extensively on gender equality, they do not publish this specific index.

- **D: World Health Organization** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Primary classification:** Governance → Government Policy Design and Implementation → Smart India Hackathon 2017

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following statements is/are correct regarding Smart India Hackathon 2017?
1. It is a centrally sponsored scheme for developing every city of our country into Smart Cities in a decade.
2. It is an initiative to identify new digital technology innovations for solving the many problems faced by our country.
3. It is a programme aimed at making all the financial transactions in our country completely digital in a decade.

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 and 3 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 3 only  
(d) 2 and 3 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Smart India Hackathon (SIH) 2017 was a nationwide initiative launched by the Ministry of Human Resource Development (now Ministry of Education) in collaboration with AICTE, i4c, and other partners. 

1. **Statement 1 is incorrect**: The Smart India Hackathon is a digital product development competition, not a scheme for urban development. The 'Smart Cities Mission' is the centrally sponsored scheme aimed at developing 100 cities into smart cities, not 'every city' in the country. 
2. **Statement 2 is correct**: SIH 2017 was specifically designed to identify new digital technology innovations by challenging students to solve 598 problem statements identified by 29 different Union Ministries and government departments. It aimed to institutionalize a model for harnessing the creativity of students for nation-building. 
3. **Statement 3 is incorrect**: While the hackathon encouraged digital solutions, its primary objective was not the complete digitization of all financial transactions in the country within a decade. That objective is more closely associated with the broader 'Digital India' mission or specific post-demonetization digital payment initiatives. 

Grounding references include official Press Information Bureau (PIB) releases from 2017 and 2018 which describe the event as the 'world's largest open innovation model' focused on solving government problems through digital solutions.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

- **A: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

- **D: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q011"></a>
## Q11. Monetary Policy Committee composition and decisions
**Primary route:** Economy → [RBI, Monetary Policy and Liquidity Management](../../topics/economy/rbi-monetary-policy-and-liquidity-management.md) → Monetary Policy Committee composition and decisions  
**Primary classification:** Economy → RBI, Monetary Policy and Liquidity Management → Monetary Policy Committee composition and decisions  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q11 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/04_RBI-Monetary-Policy-and-Liquidity-Management.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/04_RBI-Monetary-Policy-and-Liquidity-Management.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-monetary-policy-committee-composition-and-decisions`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following statements is/are correct regarding the Monetary Policy Committee (MPC)?
1. It decides the RBI's benchmark interest rates.
2. It is a 12-member body including the Governor of RBI and is reconstituted every year.
3. It functions under the chairmanship of the Union Finance Minister.

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 only  
(b) 1 and 2 only  
(c) 3 only  
(d) 2 and 3 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Monetary Policy Committee (MPC) was established under Section 45ZB of the amended Reserve Bank of India (RBI) Act, 1934, to bring transparency and accountability to the process of setting interest rates in India. 

1. **Statement 1 is correct**: The primary mandate of the MPC is to determine the policy interest rate (benchmark repo rate) required to achieve the inflation target set by the Government of India (currently 4% with a tolerance band of +/- 2%). Its decisions are binding on the RBI (Section 45ZB(3) and (4) of the RBI Act).

2. **Statement 2 is incorrect**: The MPC is a **6-member body**, not 12. It consists of three members from the RBI (the Governor, the Deputy Governor in charge of monetary policy, and one officer nominated by the Central Board) and three external members appointed by the Central Government. Furthermore, external members are appointed for a **four-year term** and are not eligible for re-appointment; thus, the committee is not reconstituted every year.

3. **Statement 3 is incorrect**: The **Governor of the Reserve Bank of India** serves as the ex-officio Chairperson of the MPC. The Union Finance Minister does not chair the committee, although the government has a role in appointing the external members through a search-cum-selection committee headed by the Cabinet Secretary.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **monetary Policy Committee composition and decisions**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

**Primary route:** Art and Culture → [Indian Music](../../topics/art-and-culture/indian-music.md) → Manipuri Sankirtana tradition  
**Primary classification:** Art and Culture → Indian Music → Manipuri Sankirtana tradition

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to Manipuri Sankirtana, consider the following statements:
1. It is a song and dance performance.
2. Cymbals are the only musical instruments used in the performance.
3. It is performed to narrate the life and deeds of Lord Krishna.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1, 2 and 3  
(b) 1 and 3 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 1 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Manipuri Sankirtana is a ritual performance involving singing, drumming, and dancing, practiced by the Meitei community in Manipur. It was inscribed on the UNESCO Representative List of the Intangible Cultural Heritage of Humanity in 2013. Statement 1 is correct as it is a composite art form of song and dance (UNESCO). Statement 2 is incorrect because the performance primarily features two main instruments: the Pung (a cylindrical drum) and the Kartal (cymbals); therefore, cymbals are not the only instruments used (CCRT). Statement 3 is correct as Sankirtana is deeply rooted in Vaishnavism and is performed to narrate the life and deeds of Lord Krishna, often marking religious occasions and life-cycle ceremonies (UNESCO, PIB).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q013"></a>
## Q13. Introduction of Ryotwari Settlement
**Primary route:** Modern History → [Economic Impact of British Rule](../../topics/modern-history/economic-impact-of-british-rule.md) → Introduction of Ryotwari Settlement  
**Primary classification:** Modern History → Economic Impact of British Rule → Introduction of Ryotwari Settlement  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q13 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/07_Economic-Impact-of-British-Rule.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/07_Economic-Impact-of-British-Rule.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-introduction-of-ryotwari-settlement`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Who among the following was/were associated with the introduction of Ryotwari Settlement in India during the British rule?
1. Lord Cornwallis
2. Alexander Read
3. Thomas Munro

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 only  
(b) 1 and 3 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 2 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Ryotwari Settlement was one of the three main land revenue systems introduced by the British in India. 1. Lord Cornwallis is primarily associated with the Permanent Settlement (Zamindari System) introduced in Bengal and Bihar in 1793 (NCERT Class 8 History, Chapter 3). 2. Captain Alexander Read first formulated the Ryotwari system and implemented it on a small scale in the Baramahal district (ceded to the British after the Third Anglo-Mysore War) in 1792 (NCERT Class 12 History, Chapter 10). 3. Thomas Munro, who served as the Governor of Madras from 1820 to 1827, refined and extended the system across the Madras Presidency. Munro argued that in South India, there were no traditional zamindars, and thus the settlement should be made directly with the cultivators (ryots) (Bipan Chandra, History of Modern India). Therefore, Alexander Read and Thomas Munro were the key figures associated with the introduction and expansion of the Ryotwari system.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q014"></a>
## Q14. Advantages of bioremediation
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Biotechnology Fundamentals](../../topics/science-and-technology/biotechnology-fundamentals-and-dbt-missions.md) → Advantages of bioremediation  
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Biotechnology Fundamentals → Advantages of bioremediation  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q14 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/13_Biotechnology-Fundamentals-and-DBT-Missions.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/13_Biotechnology-Fundamentals-and-DBT-Missions.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-advantages-of-bioremediation`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In the context of solving pollution problems, what is/are the advantage/advantages of bioremediation technique?
1. It is a technique for cleaning up pollution by enhancing the same biodegradation process that occurs in nature.
2. Any contaminant with heavy metals such as cadmium and lead can be readily and completely treated by bioremediation using microorganisms.
3. Genetic engineering can be used to create microorganisms specifically designed for bioremediation.

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Bioremediation is a waste management technique that involves the use of organisms (primarily microorganisms like bacteria and fungi) to remove or neutralize pollutants from a contaminated site. 

1. **Statement 1 is correct**: Bioremediation leverages the natural metabolic processes of microorganisms to degrade environmental contaminants into less toxic or non-toxic forms. It essentially accelerates or enhances the natural biodegradation processes that occur in the environment (e.g., through bioaugmentation or biostimulation). 

2. **Statement 2 is incorrect**: Heavy metals such as cadmium, lead, and mercury are elements and cannot be 'biodegraded' or broken down into simpler, harmless substances by microorganisms. While some microbes can transform heavy metals into less toxic oxidation states or sequester them through biosorption and bioaccumulation, they cannot 'readily and completely' treat them in the same way they degrade organic pollutants like hydrocarbons. Heavy metals are persistent and often require physical or chemical removal in addition to biological methods. 

3. **Statement 3 is correct**: Genetic engineering is a significant tool in modern bioremediation. Scientists create Genetically Engineered Microorganisms (GEMs) to enhance the efficiency of pollutant degradation. A famous example is the 'Superbug' (Pseudomonas putida) developed by Ananda Chakrabarty, designed to break down crude oil. Other examples include *Deinococcus radiodurans*, which has been engineered to treat radioactive waste and ionic mercury.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **advantages of bioremediation**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q015"></a>
## Q15. Trade Disputes Act, 1929
**Primary route:** Modern History → [Left, Peasant, Workers and States Peoples Movements](../../topics/modern-history/left-peasant-workers-and-states-peoples-movements.md) → Trade Disputes Act, 1929  
**Primary classification:** Modern History → Left, Peasant, Workers and States Peoples Movements → Trade Disputes Act, 1929  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q15 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/23_Left-Peasant-Workers-and-States-Peoples-Movements.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/23_Left-Peasant-Workers-and-States-Peoples-Movements.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-trade-disputes-act-1929`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The Trade Disputes Act of 1929 provided for

(a) the participation of workers in the management of industries.  
(b) arbitrary powers to the management to quell industrial disputes.  
(c) an intervention by the British Court in the event of a trade dispute.  
(d) a system of tribunals and a ban on strikes.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — a system of tribunals and a ban on strikes.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Trade Disputes Act of 1929 was enacted by the British colonial government in India to regulate industrial relations and curb the rising labor unrest influenced by communist and socialist ideologies. The Act had two primary features: First, it established a 'system of tribunals' by providing for the appointment of Courts of Inquiry and Boards of Conciliation to investigate and settle industrial disputes (NCERT Class 12 History; Bipan Chandra, 'India's Struggle for Independence'). Second, it imposed a 'ban on strikes' under specific conditions: it made strikes and lockouts in public utility services (such as railways, postal services, water, and electricity) illegal if conducted without prior notice. Furthermore, it prohibited 'sympathetic strikes' and strikes that were intended to coerce the government or cause severe hardship to the general public (PIB; Modern History by Bipan Chandra). Motilal Nehru famously referred to this legislation as the 'Slavery of India Bill' due to its restrictive nature on workers' rights.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: the participation of workers in the management of industries.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: arbitrary powers to the management to quell industrial disputes.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: an intervention by the British Court in the event of a trade dispute.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: a system of tribunals and a ban on strikes.** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **trade Disputes Act, 1929**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q016"></a>
## Q16. Local self-government as democratic decentralisation
**Primary route:** Governance → [Local Governance and Service Delivery](../../topics/governance/local-governance-and-service-delivery.md) → Local self-government as democratic decentralisation  
**Primary classification:** Governance → Local Governance and Service Delivery → Local self-government as democratic decentralisation  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q16 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Governance/basic/12_Local-Governance-and-Service-Delivery.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Governance/basic/12_Local-Governance-and-Service-Delivery.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `governance-local-self-government-as-democratic-decentralisation`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Local self-government can be best explained as an exercise in

(a) Federalism  
(b) Democratic decentralisation  
(c) Administrative delegation  
(d) Direct democracy  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Democratic decentralisation**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Local self-government is best explained as an exercise in **Democratic Decentralisation**. This concept involves the devolution of power, functions, and resources from the central and state governments to elected local bodies (Panchayats and Municipalities) to ensure grassroots participation in governance. 

1. **Democratic Decentralisation vs. Others**: While 'Federalism' (Option 0) describes the division of power between the Union and States, local self-government is a further extension of this principle to the third tier. 'Administrative Delegation' (Option 2) refers to the transfer of authority within a bureaucratic hierarchy, whereas local self-government involves political autonomy and elected representation. 'Direct Democracy' (Option 3) is seen in the Gram Sabha, but the overall structure of local bodies is representative in nature. 
2. **Constitutional Basis**: In India, this was formally institutionalized through the **73rd and 74th Constitutional Amendment Acts of 1992**, which gave constitutional status to rural and urban local bodies, respectively. 
3. **Objective**: The primary goal is to empower people at the local level to participate in decision-making and ensure that development is tailored to local needs, as emphasized by Mahatma Gandhi's vision of 'Gram Swaraj' (NCERT Class 11, Chapter 8).

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Federalism** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: Democratic decentralisation** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: Administrative delegation** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Direct democracy** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **local self-government as democratic decentralisation**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q017"></a>
## Q17. Indian federalism and essential federal features
**Primary route:** Polity → [Federal System](../../topics/polity/federal-system.md) → Indian federalism and essential federal features  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Federal System → Indian federalism and essential federal features  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q17 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Federal-System.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Federal-System.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-indian-federalism-and-essential-federal-features`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
With reference to the Constitution of India, the Directive Principles of State Policy constitute limitations upon
1. legislative function.
2. executive function.

Which of the above statements is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Neither 1 nor 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Directive Principles of State Policy (DPSP), enshrined in Part IV of the Constitution of India (Articles 36–51), are guidelines or 'instruments of instruction' for the State. According to Article 37, these principles are non-justiciable, meaning they are not enforceable by any court. While Article 37 specifies that they are 'fundamental in the governance of the country' and it is the 'duty of the State to apply these principles in making laws,' they do not constitute legal limitations or restrictions on the State's power. In constitutional jurisprudence, 'limitations' typically refer to Fundamental Rights (Part III), which are negative obligations that prohibit the State from infringing upon individual liberties. Conversely, DPSPs are positive obligations that urge the State to take action toward socio-economic justice. Therefore, they do not act as a check or limitation on either the legislative function (law-making) or the executive function (policy implementation). This distinction is a core concept in Indian Polity, as highlighted in M. Laxmikanth's 'Indian Polity' and NCERT textbooks (M. Laxmikanth, Chapter 8; NCERT Class 11, Chapter 2).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **indian federalism and essential federal features**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

## Q18. European Union Digital Single Market Strategy
**Primary route:** Economy → [Services, Digital Economy, Fintech and Platform Markets](../../topics/economy/services-digital-economy-fintech-and-platform-markets.md) → European Union Digital Single Market Strategy  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Services, Digital Economy, Fintech and Platform Markets → European Union Digital Single Market Strategy  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q18 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/24_Services-Digital-Economy-Fintech-and-Platform-Markets.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/24_Services-Digital-Economy-Fintech-and-Platform-Markets.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-european-union-digital-single-market-strategy`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The term 'Digital Single Market Strategy' seen in the news refers to

(a) ASEAN  
(b) BRICS  
(c) EU  
(d) G20  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — EU**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The term 'Digital Single Market Strategy' refers to a policy initiative of the European Union (EU). It was officially adopted by the European Commission on May 6, 2015, under the leadership of Jean-Claude Juncker. The strategy's primary objective is to ensure that the EU's single market is fit for the digital age by moving from 28 fragmented national digital markets to a single, unified one. This initiative aims to remove virtual borders, boost digital connectivity, and facilitate seamless cross-border online activities for consumers and businesses. The strategy is built upon three main pillars: (1) Improving access for consumers and businesses to digital goods and services across Europe; (2) Creating the right conditions and a level playing field for digital networks and innovative services to flourish; and (3) Maximizing the growth potential of the European digital economy. By harmonizing regulations on issues like e-commerce, copyright, and data protection, the EU seeks to enhance its global competitiveness and foster innovation.

- **C: EU** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: G20** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q019"></a>
## Q19. Chandipur's receding-sea phenomenon
**Primary route:** Geography → [Indian Coast, Coastal Resources and Hazards](../../topics/geography/indian-coast-coastal-resources-and-hazards.md) → Chandipur's receding-sea phenomenon  
**Primary classification:** Geography → Indian Coast, Coastal Resources and Hazards → Chandipur's receding-sea phenomenon  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q19 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/10_Coastal-Landforms.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/10_Coastal-Landforms.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `geography-chandipur-s-receding-sea-phenomenon`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

At one of the places in India, if you stand on the seashore and watch the sea, you will find that the sea water recedes from the shore line a few kilometres and comes back to the shore, twice a day, and you can actually walk on the sea floor when the water recedes. This unique phenomenon is seen at

(a) Bhavnagar  
(b) Bheemunipatnam  
(c) Chandipur  
(d) Nagapattinam  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Chandipur**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The unique phenomenon described in the question is observed at Chandipur Beach, located in the Balasore district of Odisha. Known as the 'Hide and Seek' beach or the 'Vanishing Sea', the water here recedes by 2 to 5 kilometers during low tide (ebb tide) and returns during high tide twice a day (Times of India, 2022; Tripoto). This occurs due to the extremely shallow gradient of the coastline and the gravitational pull of the moon and sun (Indrosphere, 2025; NewsClick, 2025). When the sea recedes, the exposed seabed allows visitors to literally walk on the ocean floor and observe unique marine biodiversity, such as the endangered horseshoe crabs and red crabs (Gulf News, 2018; Times of India, 2025). Chandipur is also significant as it houses the Integrated Test Range (ITR) of the Defence Research and Development Organisation (DRDO) for missile testing (Edukemy, 2024). While other places like Bhavnagar have high tidal ranges, the specific 'walking on the sea floor' experience for several kilometers is the defining characteristic of Chandipur.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Bhavnagar** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: Bheemunipatnam** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Chandipur** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: Nagapattinam** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q020"></a>
## Q20. Prohibition of Benami Property Transactions Act
**Primary route:** Economy → [Taxation, GST and Fiscal Federalism](../../topics/economy/taxation-gst-finance-commission-and-fiscal-federalism.md) → Prohibition of Benami Property Transactions Act  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Taxation, GST and Fiscal Federalism → Prohibition of Benami Property Transactions Act  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q20 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/10_Taxation-GST-Finance-Commission-and-Fiscal-Federalism.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/10_Taxation-GST-Finance-Commission-and-Fiscal-Federalism.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-prohibition-of-benami-property-transactions-act`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to the 'Prohibition of Benami Property Transactions Act, 1988 (PBPT Act)', consider the following statements:
1. A property transaction is not treated as a benami transaction if the owner of the property is not aware of the transaction.
2. Properties held benami are liable for confiscation by the Government.
3. The Act provides for three authorities for investigations but does not provide for any appellate mechanism.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 2 and 3 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Prohibition of Benami Property Transactions Act, 1988 (PBPT Act), as amended by the Benami Transactions (Prohibition) Amendment Act, 2016, provides a comprehensive framework to curb benami transactions. 

1. **Statement 1 is incorrect**: According to Section 2(9)(C) of the Act, a 'benami transaction' specifically includes a transaction or an arrangement in respect of a property where the owner of the property is not aware of, or denies knowledge of, such ownership. This provision is designed to prevent the use of fictitious names or the names of unsuspecting individuals (like domestic help or the poor) to hide the real beneficial owner's identity (India Code, Section 2(9)).

2. **Statement 2 is correct**: Section 5 of the Act explicitly states that any property which is the subject matter of a benami transaction shall be liable to be confiscated by the Central Government. This confiscation occurs without the payment of any compensation to the parties involved (Income Tax Department, Section 5).

3. **Statement 3 is incorrect**: The Act provides for four authorities for its implementation: the Initiating Officer, the Approving Authority, the Administrator, and the Adjudicating Authority (Section 7). Furthermore, the Act explicitly provides for an appellate mechanism. Section 30 mandates the establishment of an Appellate Tribunal to hear appeals against the orders of the Adjudicating Authority. Any person aggrieved by an order of the Appellate Tribunal can further appeal to the High Court (PRS Legislative Research; India Code, Section 30 & 49).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **prohibition of Benami Property Transactions Act**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q021"></a>
## Q21. Ecological consequences of butterfly decline
**Primary route:** Environment and Ecology → [Ecosystem Structure and Function](../../topics/environment/ecosystem-structure-and-function.md) → Ecological consequences of butterfly decline  
**Primary classification:** Environment and Ecology → Ecosystem Structure and Function → Ecological consequences of butterfly decline  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q21 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/01_Ecosystem-Structure-and-Function.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/01_Ecosystem-Structure-and-Function.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-and-ecology-ecological-consequences-of-butterfly-decline`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Due to some reasons, if there is a huge fall in the population of species of butterflies, what could be its likely consequence/consequences?
1. Pollination of some plants could be adversely affected.
2. There could be a drastic increase in the fungal infections of some cultivated plants.
3. It could lead to a fall in the population of some species of wasps, spiders and birds.

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The question explores the ecological role of butterflies and the consequences of their population decline. 

1. **Statement 1 is correct**: Butterflies are essential pollinators for many flowering plants and crops. They transfer pollen while feeding on nectar, which is vital for the reproduction of approximately 60–70% of plant species and a significant portion of global crop production. A decline in their population would directly reduce pollination efficiency (NCERT Class 12 Biology, Chapter 14: Ecosystem; UPSC Prelims 2017 Official Answer Key).

2. **Statement 2 is incorrect**: There is no established scientific evidence or direct ecological correlation suggesting that a fall in butterfly populations leads to a 'drastic increase' in fungal infections in plants. Fungal infections are primarily driven by environmental factors like humidity, temperature, and specific pathogens. In some cases, insects actually act as vectors for fungi, meaning their decline might theoretically reduce the spread of certain fungi rather than increase infections (NCERT Class 12 Biology; Research on Plant-Insect-Fungus interactions).

3. **Statement 3 is correct**: Butterflies and their larvae (caterpillars) are a critical component of the food web. They serve as a primary food source for various predators, including specific species of wasps, spiders, and birds. A significant reduction in the prey population (butterflies) would lead to a corresponding decline in the populations of these predators due to food scarcity (NCERT Class 12 Biology, Chapter 13: Organisms and Populations; National Wildlife Federation).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **ecological consequences of butterfly decline**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q022"></a>
## Q22. Limitations of algae-based biofuels
**Primary route:** Environment and Ecology → [Renewable Energy and Green Hydrogen](../../topics/environment/renewable-energy-and-green-hydrogen.md) → Limitations of algae-based biofuels  
**Primary classification:** Environment and Ecology → Renewable Energy and Green Hydrogen → Limitations of algae-based biofuels  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q22 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/25_Renewable-Energy-and-Green-Hydrogen.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/25_Renewable-Energy-and-Green-Hydrogen.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-and-ecology-limitations-of-algae-based-biofuels`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

It is possible to produce algae based biofuels, but what is/are the likely limitation(s) of developing countries in promoting this industry?
1. Production of algae based biofuels is possible in seas only and not on continents.
2. Setting up and engineering the algae based biofuel production requires high level of expertise/technology until the construction is completed.
3. Economically viable production necessitates the setting up of large scale facilities which may raise ecological and social concerns.

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The production of algae-based biofuels (third-generation biofuels) is a complex process with specific limitations for developing countries. 

1. **Statement 1 is incorrect**: Algae-based biofuels can be produced in a variety of environments, including freshwater ponds, saline water, and wastewater on land (continents), as well as in marine environments (seas). They do not require arable land and can be grown in closed photobioreactors or open raceway ponds on non-cropland. 

2. **Statement 2 is correct**: The technology for algae-based biofuels is still largely in the pre-commercial or pilot stage. Setting up these facilities requires sophisticated engineering, high-level expertise in biotechnology (for strain selection and genetic modification), and advanced infrastructure for cultivation and lipid extraction. Developing countries often lack the specialized technical workforce and high-end technology required for the initial construction and optimization of these systems.

3. **Statement 3 is correct**: For algae biofuels to be cost-competitive with fossil fuels, they must be produced at a very large scale to achieve economies of scale. However, large-scale production can lead to significant ecological concerns, such as high water consumption (due to evaporation in open ponds), nutrient runoff (nitrogen and phosphorus), and potential biodiversity risks if genetically modified strains escape. Social concerns may also arise regarding the diversion of resources like water or land, even if the land is non-arable, which could impact local communities.

Grounding References: 
- NCERT Class 12 Biology, Chapter 15 (Biodiversity and Conservation) and general scientific principles of biotechnology.
- PIB (Press Information Bureau) releases on the National Policy on Biofuels.
- Reports from the Ministry of New and Renewable Energy (MNRE) on Third Generation Biofuels.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **limitations of algae-based biofuels**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.
