### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following are the objectives of 'National Nutrition Mission'?
1. To create awareness relating to malnutrition among pregnant women and lactating mothers.
2. To reduce the incidence of anaemia among young children, adolescent girls and women.
3. To promote the consumption of millets, coarse cereals and unpolished rice.
4. To promote the consumption of poultry eggs.

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 1, 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1, 2 and 4 only  
(d) 3 and 4 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 and 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The National Nutrition Mission (NNM), which was later rebranded and expanded as POSHAN Abhiyaan, was designed to address the high prevalence of malnutrition in India through a multi-sectoral approach. 

1. **Statement 1 is correct**: A primary objective of the mission is to create awareness regarding malnutrition among pregnant women and lactating mothers through Information, Education, and Communication (IEC) campaigns and 'Jan Andolan' (People's Movement) (PIB, 2014; PIB, 2017).
2. **Statement 2 is correct**: The mission set specific, quantifiable targets to reduce the incidence of anaemia among young children (6-59 months), adolescent girls, and women (15-49 years) by 3% per annum (Ministry of Women and Child Development; PIB, 2018).
3. **Statement 3 is incorrect**: While the government promotes millets and coarse cereals under the National Food Security Mission (NFSM) and other agricultural initiatives, it was not a specific stated objective of the National Nutrition Mission framework at the time of the 2017 examination.
4. **Statement 4 is incorrect**: Promoting the consumption of poultry eggs is not an objective of the NNM. Although eggs are included in the supplementary nutrition menu of the Integrated Child Development Services (ICDS) in several states, the NNM itself focuses on monitoring, convergence, and specific outcome targets like reducing stunting, wasting, and anaemia (PIB, 2017).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **4: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: 1, 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 1, 2 and 4 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 3 and 4 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **national Nutrition Mission objectives**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q024"></a>
## Q24. Factories Act, 1881 and early labour movement
**Primary route:** Modern History → [Left, Peasant, Workers and States Peoples Movements](../../topics/modern-history/left-peasant-workers-and-states-peoples-movements.md) → Factories Act, 1881 and early labour movement  
**Primary classification:** Modern History → Left, Peasant, Workers and States Peoples Movements → Factories Act, 1881 and early labour movement  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q24 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/23_Left-Peasant-Workers-and-States-Peoples-Movements.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/23_Left-Peasant-Workers-and-States-Peoples-Movements.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-factories-act-1881-and-early-labour-movement`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. The Factories Act, 1881 was passed with a view to fix the wages of industrial workers and to allow the workers to form trade unions.
2. N.M. Lokhande was a pioneer in organizing the labour movement in British India.

Which of the above statements is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect. The first Indian Factories Act was passed in 1881 during the viceroyalty of Lord Ripon. However, its scope was very limited; it primarily focused on child labor in factories employing more than 100 workers. It prohibited the employment of children under the age of 7, limited the working hours of children aged 7 to 12 to nine hours a day, and mandated four holidays a month. It did not address the fixing of wages for industrial workers, nor did it provide for the formation of trade unions (Spectrum: A Brief History of Modern India, Chapter 31). Statement 2 is correct. Narayan Meghaji Lokhande (1848–1897) was a pioneer of the labor movement in India. A close associate of Mahatma Jyotirao Phule, he founded the Bombay Mill Hands Association in 1890, which is considered the first labor organization in British India. He also edited the Marathi periodical 'Deenbandhu' to highlight the grievances of the working class (Bipin Chandra: India's Struggle for Independence, Chapter 17).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **factories Act, 1881 and early labour movement**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q025"></a>
## Q25. Potential sites for carbon sequestration
**Primary route:** Environment and Ecology → [Climate Change Science](../../topics/environment/climate-change-science-greenhouse-effect.md) → Potential sites for carbon sequestration  
**Primary classification:** Environment and Ecology → Climate Change Science → Potential sites for carbon sequestration  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q25 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/17_Climate-Change-Science-Greenhouse-Effect.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/17_Climate-Change-Science-Greenhouse-Effect.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-and-ecology-potential-sites-for-carbon-sequestration`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In the context of mitigating the impending global warming due to anthropogenic emissions of carbon dioxide, which of the following can be the potential sites for carbon sequestration?
1. Abandoned and uneconomic coal seams
2. Depleted oil and gas reservoirs
3. Subterranean deep saline formations

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Carbon sequestration is the process of capturing and storing atmospheric carbon dioxide (CO2) to mitigate global warming. Geological sequestration involves injecting CO2 into deep underground formations. 
1. **Abandoned and uneconomic coal seams:** CO2 can be injected into coal seams where it is adsorbed onto the coal surface. This process can also displace methane, leading to Enhanced Coal Bed Methane (ECBM) recovery while securely storing the CO2 (IPCC Special Report on CCS, Chapter 5; Source [2], [6]).
2. **Depleted oil and gas reservoirs:** These formations have a proven ability to trap fluids and gases for millions of years. They are ideal for sequestration because their geology is well-understood and they often have existing infrastructure. CO2 injection is also used for Enhanced Oil Recovery (EOR) (IPCC Special Report on CCS, Chapter 5; Source [7], [10]).
3. **Subterranean deep saline formations:** These are porous rock formations containing highly saline water (brine). They are widely distributed and offer the largest potential capacity for CO2 storage globally due to their vast pore volumes (IPCC Special Report on CCS, Chapter 5; Source [3], [12]).
All three are recognized as viable geological storage options by the Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change (IPCC).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **potential sites for carbon sequestration**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q026"></a>
## Q26. Butler Committee, 1927
**Primary route:** Modern History → [Colonial Government Structure and Constitutional Development](../../topics/modern-history/colonial-government-structure-and-constitutional-development.md) → Butler Committee, 1927  
**Primary classification:** Modern History → Colonial Government Structure and Constitutional Development → Butler Committee, 1927  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q26 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/12_Administrative-Changes-After-1858.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/12_Administrative-Changes-After-1858.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-butler-committee-1927`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The object of the Butler Committee of 1927 was to

(a) Define the jurisdiction of the Central and Provincial Governments.  
(b) Define the powers of the Secretary of State for India.  
(c) Impose censorship on national press.  
(d) Improve the relationship between the Government of India and the Indian States.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Improve the relationship between the Government of India and the Indian States.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Butler Committee, formally known as the **Indian States Committee**, was appointed in 1927 under the chairmanship of Sir Harcourt Butler. Its primary objective was to investigate and clarify the relationship between the British Paramount Power (the Crown) and the Indian Princely States, and to suggest ways to improve the relationship between the Government of India and these States (Source: Spectrum's A Brief History of Modern India, Chapter 24). The committee was formed in response to the concerns of the Princely States regarding their status in a future constitutional setup where British India might gain self-governance. In its 1929 report, the committee famously reaffirmed the doctrine of **Paramountcy**, stating that 'Paramountcy must remain paramount' and must adapt to the shifting necessities of time. It also recommended that the Viceroy, rather than the Governor-General in Council, should act as the agent of the Crown in dealings with the States to ensure that the States were not handed over to an Indian government responsible to an elected legislature without the rulers' consent (Source: NCERT Class 12 History, Themes in Indian History Part III).

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Define the jurisdiction of the Central and Provincial Governments.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: Define the powers of the Secretary of State for India.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Impose censorship on national press.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Improve the relationship between the Government of India and the Indian States.** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **butler Committee, 1927**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q027"></a>
## Q27. Domestic Content Requirement in solar power
**Primary route:** Economy → [Foreign Trade, WTO, FTAs and Protectionism](../../topics/economy/foreign-trade-wto-ftas-and-protectionism.md) → Domestic Content Requirement in solar power  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Foreign Trade, WTO, FTAs and Protectionism → Domestic Content Requirement in solar power  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q27 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/20_Foreign-Trade-WTO-FTAs-and-Protectionism.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/20_Foreign-Trade-WTO-FTAs-and-Protectionism.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-domestic-content-requirement-in-solar-power`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The term 'Domestic Content Requirement' is sometimes seen in the news with reference to

(a) Developing solar power production in our country  
(b) Granting licences to foreign T.V. channels in our country  
(c) Exporting our food products to other countries  
(d) Permitting foreign educational institutions to set up their campuses in our country  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — Developing solar power production in our country**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The term 'Domestic Content Requirement' (DCR) refers to a policy mandate where a government requires that a certain percentage of the components or value of a product be sourced from local manufacturers. In India, this term gained prominence in the context of the Jawaharlal Nehru National Solar Mission (JNNSM), where the government required solar power developers to use solar cells and modules manufactured in India for specific projects (PIB, 2014). This policy was intended to promote the domestic solar manufacturing industry. However, the United States challenged this measure at the World Trade Organization (WTO), claiming it violated 'national treatment' obligations by discriminating against imported solar products. In 2016, the WTO Dispute Settlement Body ruled against India, stating that the DCR measures were inconsistent with Article III:4 of the GATT 1994 and Article 2.1 of the TRIMs Agreement (WTO Dispute DS456).

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Developing solar power production in our country** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: Granting licences to foreign T.V. channels in our country** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Exporting our food products to other countries** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Permitting foreign educational institutions to set up their campuses in our country** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **domestic Content Requirement in solar power**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

## Q28. Nuclear Security Summits and nuclear-material governance
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Nuclear Power and Security](../../topics/science-and-technology/nuclear-power-and-three-stage-programme.md) → Nuclear Security Summits and nuclear-material governance  
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Nuclear Power and Security → Nuclear Security Summits and nuclear-material governance  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q28 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/04_Nuclear-Power-and-Three-Stage-Programme.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/04_Nuclear-Power-and-Three-Stage-Programme.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-nuclear-security-summits-and-nuclear-material-governance`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. The Nuclear Security Summits are periodically held under the aegis of the United Nations.
2. The International Panel on Fissile Materials is an organ of International Atomic Energy Agency.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Neither 1 nor 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect because the Nuclear Security Summits (NSS) were a series of world summits aimed at preventing nuclear terrorism, initiated by U.S. President Barack Obama in 2010. They were not held under the aegis of the United Nations; instead, they were a separate international initiative hosted by individual countries (USA in 2010 and 2016, South Korea in 2012, and the Netherlands in 2014). While the UN and IAEA participated, they did not organize the summits (Source: Arms Control Association, 'Nuclear Security Summit at a Glance'). Statement 2 is incorrect because the International Panel on Fissile Materials (IPFM) is an independent, non-governmental group of arms-control and non-proliferation experts from 17 countries. It was established in 2006 and is hosted by the Program on Science and Global Security at Princeton University. It is not an organ or an arm of the International Atomic Energy Agency (IAEA), which is an autonomous international organization within the United Nations system (Source: International Panel on Fissile Materials official website, 'About IPFM').

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.

## Q29. Eligibility for National Pension System
**Primary route:** Economy → [Securities, Bonds, Equity, Derivatives and Investment Funds](../../topics/economy/securities-bonds-equity-derivatives-and-investment-funds.md) → Eligibility for National Pension System  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Securities, Bonds, Equity, Derivatives and Investment Funds → Eligibility for National Pension System  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q29 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/08_Securities-Bonds-Equity-Derivatives-and-Investment-Funds.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/08_Securities-Bonds-Equity-Derivatives-and-Investment-Funds.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-eligibility-for-national-pension-system`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Who among the following can join the National Pension System (NPS)?

(a) Resident Indian citizens only  
(b) Persons of age from 21 to 55 only  
(c) All State Government employees joining the services after the date of notification by the respective State Governments  
(d) All Central Government employees including those of Armed Forces joining the services on or after 1st April, 2004  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — All State Government employees joining the services after the date of notification by the respective State Governments**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The National Pension System (NPS) is a government-sponsored pension scheme in India. The correct option is 2 because NPS is mandatory for all State Government employees joining the services after the date of notification by their respective State Governments. Most states (except West Bengal) adopted NPS for their employees on various dates following the Central Government's implementation in 2004 (PFRDA, NPS Trust). 

Analysis of other options:
- Option 0 is incorrect because NPS is open to both Resident Indian citizens and Non-Resident Indians (NRIs). Overseas Citizens of India (OCI) were also made eligible in 2019 (PIB, PFRDA Circular 2019).
- Option 1 is incorrect because the entry age for NPS is 18 to 70 years (it was 18 to 60 years at the time of the 2017 exam and later increased to 65 and then 70) (PFRDA Circular 2021).
- Option 3 is incorrect because the Armed Forces are specifically excluded from the NPS. Additionally, the mandatory date for Central Government employees was January 1, 2004, not April 1, 2004 (Department of Financial Services, Ministry of Finance).

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Resident Indian citizens only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: Persons of age from 21 to 55 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: All State Government employees joining the services after the date of notification by the respective State Governments** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: All Central Government employees including those of Armed Forces joining the services on or after 1st April, 2004** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **eligibility for National Pension System**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q030"></a>
## Q30. Teesta river geography
**Primary route:** Geography → [India Drainage and River Systems](../../topics/geography/india-drainage-and-river-interlinking.md) → Teesta river geography  
**Primary classification:** Geography → India Drainage and River Systems → Teesta river geography  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q30 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/36_Contemporary-Geographical-Issues-India.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/36_Contemporary-Geographical-Issues-India.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `geography-teesta-river-geography`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to river Teesta, consider the following statements:
1. The source of river Teesta is the same as that of Brahmaputra but it flows through Sikkim.
2. River Rangeet originates in Sikkim and it is a tributary of river Teesta.
3. River Teesta flows into Bay of Bengal on the border of India and Bangladesh.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 3 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The correct answer is '2 only' based on the following geographical facts:

1. **Statement 1 is incorrect**: The Teesta River originates from the **Pahunri (or Teesta Khangse) glacier** and the **Tso Lhamo Lake** in North Sikkim at an elevation of about 5,280 meters. In contrast, the Brahmaputra River (known as the Yarlung Tsangpo in Tibet) originates from the **Angsi Glacier** (or Chemayungdung Glacier) near Mount Kailash in the Tibet Autonomous Region. Thus, they do not share the same source (NCERT Class 11 Geography, Chapter 3; Britannica).

2. **Statement 2 is correct**: The **Rangeet River** originates in the Himalayan mountains of **West Sikkim** (specifically from the Rathong Glacier). It is the largest right-bank tributary of the Teesta River and meets it at a confluence known as Tribeni near Melli (Sikkim Tourism; Wikipedia).

3. **Statement 3 is incorrect**: The Teesta River flows through Sikkim and West Bengal in India before entering Bangladesh. In Bangladesh, it joins the **Brahmaputra (Jamuna) River** at Phulchhari. The combined waters then merge with the Padma (Ganga) and finally the Meghna before emptying into the Bay of Bengal. It does not flow directly into the Bay of Bengal on the India-Bangladesh border (Drishti IAS; Britannica).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q031"></a>
## Q31. Zika virus transmission
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [General Science: Biology and Physiology](../../topics/science-and-technology/general-science-biology-and-physiology.md) → Zika virus transmission  
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → General Science: Biology and Physiology → Zika virus transmission  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q31 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/23_General-Science-Biology-and-Physiology.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/23_General-Science-Biology-and-Physiology.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-zika-virus-transmission`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. In tropical regions, Zika virus disease is transmitted by the same mosquito that transmits dengue.
2. Sexual transmission of Zika virus disease is possible.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Both 1 and 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct: Zika virus is primarily transmitted to humans through the bite of an infected mosquito from the Aedes genus, specifically Aedes aegypti and Aedes albopictus. These are the same mosquito species responsible for transmitting other viral diseases such as dengue, chikungunya, and yellow fever (WHO, 2024; CDC, 2025). Statement 2 is correct: While mosquito bites are the primary mode of transmission, Zika virus can also be spread through sexual contact. It has been detected in semen, vaginal fluids, and other bodily fluids, and transmission can occur from an infected person to their partners through vaginal, anal, or oral sex, even if the infected person is asymptomatic at the time (CDC, 2025; WHO, 2024). Other modes of transmission include mother-to-fetus during pregnancy and blood transfusions.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q032"></a>
## Q32. BIS hallmark, AGMARK and food standards
**Primary route:** Governance → [Regulatory Governance and Independent Regulators](../../topics/governance/regulatory-governance-and-independent-regulators.md) → BIS hallmark, AGMARK and food standards  
**Primary classification:** Governance → Regulatory Governance and Independent Regulators → BIS hallmark, AGMARK and food standards  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q32 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Governance/basic/11_Regulatory-Governance-and-Independent-Regulators.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Governance/basic/11_Regulatory-Governance-and-Independent-Regulators.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `governance-bis-hallmark-agmark-and-food-standards`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. The Standard Mark of Bureau of Indian Standards (BIS) is mandatory for automotive tyres and tubes.
2. AGMARK is a quality Certification Mark issued by the Food and Agriculture Organisation (FAO).

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct: The Bureau of Indian Standards (BIS) certification is mandatory for automotive tyres and tubes in India. This was mandated under the 'Pneumatic Tyres and Tubes for Automotive Vehicles (Quality Control) Order, 2009', which requires all such products to conform to specified Indian Standards (like IS 15633 for passenger cars and IS 15636 for commercial vehicles) and bear the ISI mark (BIS Standard Mark). This regulation became fully effective on May 13, 2011, to ensure consumer safety and product quality. Statement 2 is incorrect: AGMARK is a quality certification mark for agricultural products in India, but it is issued by the Directorate of Marketing and Inspection (DMI), an agency under the Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare, Government of India. It is not issued by the Food and Agriculture Organisation (FAO), which is an international body under the United Nations. AGMARK is legally enforced by the Agricultural Produce (Grading and Marking) Act, 1937.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q033"></a>
## Q33. National Agriculture Market advantages
**Primary route:** Economy → [APMC, e-NAM, FPOs and Agricultural Supply Chains](../../topics/economy/apmc-e-nam-fpos-and-agricultural-supply-chains.md) → National Agriculture Market advantages  
**Primary classification:** Economy → APMC, e-NAM, FPOs and Agricultural Supply Chains → National Agriculture Market advantages  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q33 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/13_APMC-e-NAM-FPOs-and-Agricultural-Supply-Chains.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/13_APMC-e-NAM-FPOs-and-Agricultural-Supply-Chains.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-national-agriculture-market-advantages`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

What is/are the advantage/advantages of implementing the 'National Agriculture Market' scheme?
1. It is a pan-India electronic trading portal for agricultural commodities.
2. It provides the farmers access to nationwide market, with prices commensurate with the quality of their produce.

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Both 1 and 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The National Agriculture Market (eNAM) is a pan-India electronic trading portal launched by the Government of India on April 14, 2016. 

1. **Statement 1 is correct**: eNAM is a pan-India electronic trading portal that networks existing Agricultural Produce Market Committee (APMC) mandis to create a unified national market for agricultural commodities. It is managed by the Small Farmers Agribusiness Consortium (SFAC) under the Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers' Welfare (Source: Official eNAM Portal, 'About NAM').

2. **Statement 2 is correct**: One of the core objectives of eNAM is to provide farmers with access to a nationwide market, reducing the number of intermediaries and ensuring better price discovery. The platform integrates quality testing (assaying) of the produce, which helps farmers receive prices that are commensurate with the quality of their goods. This transparency in the bidding process and quality-based pricing are key advantages of the scheme (Source: PIB Release, 'e-NAM: A National Agriculture Market').

Therefore, both statements are correct.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q034"></a>
## Q34. National Intellectual Property Rights Policy
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Intellectual Property Rights and Patents](../../topics/science-and-technology/intellectual-property-rights-and-patents.md) → National Intellectual Property Rights Policy  
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Intellectual Property Rights and Patents → National Intellectual Property Rights Policy  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q34 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/17_Intellectual-Property-Rights-and-Patents.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/17_Intellectual-Property-Rights-and-Patents.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-national-intellectual-property-rights-policy`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to the 'National Intellectual Property Rights Policy', consider the following statements:
1. It reiterates India's commitment to the Doha Development Agenda and the TRIPS Agreement.
2. Department of Industrial Policy and Promotion is the nodal agency for regulating intellectual property rights in India.

Which of the above statements is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Both 1 and 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct: The National Intellectual Property Rights (IPR) Policy, approved by the Union Cabinet on May 12, 2016, explicitly states that India remains committed to the World Trade Organization (WTO) Agreement on Trade-Related Aspects of Intellectual Property Rights (TRIPS) and the Doha Development Agenda. The policy document notes that India has a well-established TRIPS-compliant legislative, administrative, and judicial framework to safeguard IPRs while utilizing the flexibilities provided in the international regime to address its developmental concerns, such as public health (PIB, May 13, 2016).

Statement 2 is correct: The Department of Industrial Policy and Promotion (DIPP), which is under the Ministry of Commerce and Industry, was designated as the nodal agency for regulating, coordinating, and overseeing the implementation of IPRs in India. Following the 2016 policy, the administration of the Copyright Act, 1957, and the Semiconductor Integrated Circuits Layout-Design Act, 2000, was transferred to DIPP to ensure an integrated approach and synergy between different IP offices (PIB, Dec 27, 2018). Note: DIPP was renamed to the Department for Promotion of Industry and Internal Trade (DPIIT) in 2019, but it remains the nodal agency.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q035"></a>
## Q35. Protected animals under Wildlife Protection Act
**Primary route:** Environment and Ecology → [Wildlife Protection Act and Schedules](../../topics/environment/wildlife-protection-act-and-schedules.md) → Protected animals under Wildlife Protection Act  
**Primary classification:** Environment and Ecology → Wildlife Protection Act and Schedules → Protected animals under Wildlife Protection Act  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q35 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/08_Wildlife-Protection-Act-and-Schedules.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/08_Wildlife-Protection-Act-and-Schedules.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-and-ecology-protected-animals-under-wildlife-protection-act`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

According to the Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972, which of the following animals cannot be hunted by any person except under some provisions provided by law?
1. Gharial
2. Indian wild ass
3. Wild buffalo

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

According to the Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972, animals listed in Schedule I, II, III, and IV are protected from hunting under Section 9. Specifically, animals in Schedule I and Part II of Schedule II receive the highest level of protection. The animals mentioned in the question—Gharial (Schedule I, Part II, Entry 14), Indian wild ass (Schedule I, Part I, Entry 22), and Wild buffalo (Schedule I, Part I, Entry 41)—are all listed under Schedule I. Section 9 of the Act states that 'No person shall hunt any wild animal specified in Schedules I, II, III and IV except as provided under section 11 and section 12.' Section 11 allows the Chief Wild Life Warden to permit hunting if an animal becomes dangerous to human life or is disabled/diseased beyond recovery. Section 12 allows for permits for special purposes such as education, scientific research, and collection of specimens for zoos. Thus, all three animals cannot be hunted except under these specific legal provisions.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q036"></a>
## Q36. Nature and enforceability of Fundamental Duties
**Primary route:** Polity → [Fundamental Duties](../../topics/polity/salient-features-and-constitutional-parts.md) → Nature and enforceability of Fundamental Duties  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Fundamental Duties → Nature and enforceability of Fundamental Duties  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q36 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Fundamental-Duties.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Fundamental-Duties.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-nature-and-enforceability-of-fundamental-duties`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following statements is/are true of the Fundamental Duties of an Indian citizen?
1. A legislative process has been provided to enforce these duties.
2. They are correlative to legal duties.

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Neither 1 nor 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect: Fundamental Duties, listed in Article 51A (Part IV-A) of the Indian Constitution, are non-justiciable in nature. This means that the Constitution does not provide a direct legislative process or a specific mechanism for their enforcement by the courts. While the Parliament is free to enforce them by enacting suitable legislation (such as the Prevention of Insults to National Honour Act, 1971, or the Wildlife Protection Act, 1972), the 'legislative process' is not a built-in constitutional provision for these duties (Source: M. Laxmikanth, Indian Polity, Chapter on Fundamental Duties). Statement 2 is incorrect: Fundamental Duties are moral and civic obligations of citizens. In jurisprudence, rights and duties are often described as correlative; however, Fundamental Duties are not 'correlative to legal duties.' Legal duties are those that are legally enforceable and carry sanctions for non-compliance, whereas Fundamental Duties are non-justiciable and do not automatically carry legal penalties unless specifically codified into law (Source: NCERT Class 11, Indian Constitution at Work, Chapter 2). Therefore, neither statement is true.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **nature and enforceability of Fundamental Duties**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q037"></a>
## Q37. Early political associations and founders
**Primary route:** Modern History → [National Movement Organisations and Leaders](../../topics/modern-history/national-movement-organisations-and-leaders.md) → Early political associations and founders  
**Primary classification:** Modern History → National Movement Organisations and Leaders → Early political associations and founders  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q37 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/14_Foundation-of-INC-and-Moderate-Phase.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/14_Foundation-of-INC-and-Moderate-Phase.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-early-political-associations-and-founders`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following pairs:
1. Radhakanta Deb — First President of the British Indian Association
2. Gazulu Lakshminarasu Chetty — Founder of the Madras Mahajana Sabha
3. Surendranath Banerjee — Founder of the Indian Association

Which of the above pairs is/are correctly matched?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 1 and 3 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 1 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The question evaluates knowledge of socio-political organizations in pre-independence India. 
1. **Radhakanta Deb — First President of the British Indian Association**: This pair is correct. The British Indian Association was established on October 29, 1851, in Calcutta. Raja Radhakanta Deb served as its first President, while Debendranath Tagore was its first Secretary (Source: NCERT Class 12 History, Themes in Indian History Part III; Spectrum's A Brief History of Modern India). 
2. **Gazulu Lakshminarasu Chetty — Founder of the Madras Mahajana Sabha**: This pair is incorrect. Gazulu Lakshminarasu Chetty was the founder of the **Madras Native Association** in 1852. The Madras Mahajana Sabha was founded much later, in May 1884, by M. Veeraraghavachariar, G. Subramania Iyer, and P. Anandacharlu (Source: Bipin Chandra, India's Struggle for Independence; Britannica). 
3. **Surendranath Banerjee — Founder of the Indian Association**: This pair is correct. The Indian Association (also known as the Indian National Association) was founded in 1876 in Calcutta by Surendranath Banerjee and Ananda Mohan Bose to promote the political interests of the people (Source: NCERT Class 8 History, Chapter 9; Spectrum's A Brief History of Modern India). 
Therefore, only pairs 1 and 3 are correctly matched.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 1 and 3 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q038"></a>
## Q38. Preamble objectives
**Primary route:** Polity → [Preamble](../../topics/polity/salient-features-and-constitutional-parts.md) → Preamble objectives  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Preamble → Preamble objectives  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q38 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Preamble.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Preamble.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-preamble-objectives`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following objectives is not embodied in the Preamble to the Constitution of India?

(a) Liberty of thought  
(b) Economic liberty  
(c) Liberty of expression  
(d) Liberty of belief  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Economic liberty**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Preamble to the Constitution of India explicitly defines the objectives of the Indian State and the rights of its citizens. According to the text of the Preamble, it seeks to secure to all its citizens: JUSTICE (social, economic, and political); LIBERTY (of thought, expression, belief, faith, and worship); EQUALITY (of status and of opportunity); and FRATERNITY (assuring the dignity of the individual and the unity and integrity of the Nation). While 'economic' is mentioned under the objective of 'Justice', it is not associated with 'Liberty'. The five types of liberty specifically mentioned are thought, expression, belief, faith, and worship. Therefore, 'Economic liberty' is not an objective embodied in the Preamble. This distinction is highlighted in standard references such as M. Laxmikanth's 'Indian Polity' and NCERT textbooks.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Liberty of thought** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: Economic liberty** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: Liberty of expression** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Liberty of belief** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **preamble objectives**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

**Primary route:** Governance → [Regulatory Governance and Independent Regulators](../../topics/governance/regulatory-governance-and-independent-regulators.md) → Quality Council of India  
**Primary classification:** Governance → Regulatory Governance and Independent Regulators → Quality Council of India

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to 'Quality Council of India (QCI)', consider the following statements:
1. QCI was set up jointly by the Government of India and the Indian Industry.
2. Chairman of QCI is appointed by the Prime Minister on the recommendations of the industry to the Government.

Which of the above statements is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Both 1 and 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct: The Quality Council of India (QCI) was established in 1997 as an autonomous, non-profit body through a public-private partnership (PPP) model. It was set up jointly by the Government of India (represented by the Department for Promotion of Industry and Internal Trade, Ministry of Commerce and Industry) and the Indian industry, specifically the three premier industry associations: the Associated Chambers of Commerce and Industry of India (ASSOCHAM), the Confederation of Indian Industry (CII), and the Federation of Indian Chambers of Commerce and Industry (FICCI). Statement 2 is correct: The Chairman of QCI is appointed by the Prime Minister of India. This appointment is made based on the recommendations of the industry to the Government. The Chairman is typically a non-executive head and a prominent figure from the industry. For instance, Ratan Tata was the first Chairman, and more recently, Jaxay Shah was appointed to the post in 2022.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

## Q40. Purposes of Small Finance Banks
**Primary route:** Economy → [Banking Structure, NBFCs and Financial Regulation](../../topics/economy/banking-structure-nbfcs-and-financial-regulation.md) → Purposes of Small Finance Banks  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Banking Structure, NBFCs and Financial Regulation → Purposes of Small Finance Banks  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q40 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/05_Banking-Structure-NBFCs-and-Financial-Regulation.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/05_Banking-Structure-NBFCs-and-Financial-Regulation.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-purposes-of-small-finance-banks`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

What is the purpose of setting up of Small Finance Banks (SFBs) in India?
1. To supply credit to small business units
2. To supply credit to small and marginal farmers
3. To encourage young entrepreneurs to set up business particularly in rural areas.

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 and 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Small Finance Banks (SFBs) were established in India following the recommendations of the Nachiket Mor Committee on Comprehensive Financial Services for Small Businesses and Low Income Households. According to the 'Guidelines for Licensing of Small Finance Banks in the Private Sector' released by the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) on November 27, 2014, the primary objectives of setting up SFBs are to further financial inclusion by: (i) providing savings vehicles primarily to unserved and underserved sections of the population, and (ii) supplying credit to small business units, small and marginal farmers, micro and small industries, and other unorganized sector entities through high-technology, low-cost operations. Statement 1 and Statement 2 directly align with these official objectives. Statement 3, which mentions encouraging 'young entrepreneurs' specifically, is not an explicit objective mentioned in the RBI guidelines; the focus is on the underserved segments of the economy regardless of the age of the entrepreneur. Therefore, only statements 1 and 2 are correct.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

## Q41. Asia-Pacific housing and urban-development ministerial conference
**Primary route:** Governance → [Development Processes and the Development Industry](../../topics/governance/development-processes-and-the-development-industry.md) → Asia-Pacific housing and urban-development ministerial conference  
**Primary classification:** Governance → Development Processes and the Development Industry → Asia-Pacific housing and urban-development ministerial conference  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q41 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Governance/basic/03_Development-Processes-and-the-Development-Industry.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Governance/basic/03_Development-Processes-and-the-Development-Industry.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `governance-asia-pacific-housing-and-urban-development-ministerial-conference`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to 'Asia Pacific Ministerial Conference on Housing and Urban Development (APMCHUD)', consider the following statements:
1. The first APMCHUD was held in India in 2006 on the theme 'Emerging Urban Forms — Policy Responses and Governance Structure'.
2. India hosts all the Annual Ministerial Conferences in partnership with ADB, APEC and ASEAN.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Neither 1 nor 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Both statements are incorrect. 

1. **Statement 1 is incorrect**: While the first Asia Pacific Ministerial Conference on Housing and Urban Development (APMCHUD) was indeed held in New Delhi, India, in December 2006, its theme was **'A Vision for Sustainable Urbanization in the Asia-Pacific by 2020'**. The theme mentioned in the statement, 'Emerging Urban Forms — Policy Responses and Governance Structure', was actually the theme of the **6th APMCHUD** held in New Delhi in December 2016 (PIB, 2016; MoHUA). 

2. **Statement 2 is incorrect**: APMCHUD is a **biennial** (held every two years) conference, not an annual one. Furthermore, the hosting of the conference rotates among member countries in the Asia-Pacific region; India does not host all of them. Past hosts include Tehran, Iran (2008), Solo, Indonesia (2010), Amman, Jordan (2012), and Seoul, Republic of Korea (2014). While organizations like ADB, UN-Habitat, and others participate as partners, the conference is an inter-governmental mechanism supported primarily by UN-Habitat (UN-Habitat, 2006; MoHUA Guidelines).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**

- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q042"></a>
## Q42. Democracy and individual participation
**Primary route:** Polity → [Salient Features of the Constitution](../../topics/polity/salient-features-and-constitutional-parts.md) → Democracy and individual participation  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Salient Features of the Constitution → Democracy and individual participation  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q42 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Salient-Features.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Salient-Features.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-democracy-and-individual-participation`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Democracy's superior virtue lies in the fact that it calls into activity

(a) the intelligence and character of ordinary men and women.  
(b) the methods for strengthening executive leadership.  
(c) a superior individual with dynamism and vision.  
(d) a band of dedicated party workers.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — the intelligence and character of ordinary men and women.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The statement that democracy's superior virtue lies in the fact that it calls into activity 'the intelligence and character of ordinary men and women' is a classic argument in political philosophy, most notably associated with John Stuart Mill. In his work 'Considerations on Representative Government' (1861), Mill argued that the primary merit of a democratic government is its ability to promote the intellectual and moral development of its citizens. By participating in the political process—whether through voting, local administration, or public debate—ordinary individuals are forced to look beyond their immediate self-interest, consider the public good, and exercise their judgment. This active participation fosters a sense of responsibility and enhances the 'intelligence and character' of the populace (NCERT Class 11 Political Theory, Chapter 6: Citizenship). Unlike systems that rely on a 'superior individual' or 'executive leadership,' democracy empowers the masses, making it a system of self-education and human growth.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: the intelligence and character of ordinary men and women.** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: the methods for strengthening executive leadership.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: a superior individual with dynamism and vision.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: a band of dedicated party workers.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **democracy and individual participation**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q043"></a>
## Q43. Unified Payments Interface consequence
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Digital India and India Stack](../../topics/science-and-technology/digital-india-india-stack-and-digital-infrastructure.md) → Unified Payments Interface consequence  
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Digital India and India Stack → Unified Payments Interface consequence  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q43 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/08_Digital-India-and-India-Stack-UPI-Aadhaar.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/08_Digital-India-and-India-Stack-UPI-Aadhaar.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-unified-payments-interface-consequence`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following is a most likely consequence of implementing the 'Unified Payments Interface (UPI)'?

(a) Mobile wallets will not be necessary for online payments.  
(b) Digital currency will totally replace the physical currency in about two decades.  
(c) FDI inflows will drastically increase.  
(d) Direct transfer of subsidies to poor people will become very effective.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — Mobile wallets will not be necessary for online payments.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Unified Payments Interface (UPI), launched by the National Payments Corporation of India (NPCI) in 2016, is a real-time payment system that allows for instant, 24/7, inter-bank electronic fund transfers through a mobile platform. The most significant and immediate consequence of its implementation is that mobile wallets (Prepaid Payment Instruments or PPIs) are no longer a necessity for online payments. Before UPI, users often had to 'load' money from their bank accounts into a third-party digital wallet (like Paytm or Mobikwik) to facilitate quick transactions. UPI bypasses this intermediate step by allowing direct bank-to-bank transfers using a Virtual Payment Address (VPA) or mobile number, making the 'store of value' in a wallet redundant (NPCI, 'UPI Product Overview'). While UPI promotes a 'less-cash' economy, the claim that digital currency will 'totally replace' physical currency (Option 1) is an extreme and speculative prediction. FDI inflows (Option 2) are driven by broader macroeconomic policies and ease of doing business, not just a payment interface. Direct Benefit Transfer (Option 3) is primarily facilitated through the Aadhaar Bridge Payment System (ABPS) and Aadhaar-Enabled Payment System (AePS), which were already functional before UPI's widespread adoption (Economic Survey 2016-17).

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Mobile wallets will not be necessary for online payments.** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: Digital currency will totally replace the physical currency in about two decades.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: FDI inflows will drastically increase.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Direct transfer of subsidies to poor people will become very effective.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **unified Payments Interface consequence**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q044"></a>
## Q44. Event horizon, singularity, string theory and Standard Model
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [General Science: Physics Fundamentals](../../topics/science-and-technology/general-science-physics-fundamentals.md) → Event horizon, singularity, string theory and Standard Model  
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → General Science: Physics Fundamentals → Event horizon, singularity, string theory and Standard Model  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q44 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/21_General-Science-Physics-Fundamentals.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/21_General-Science-Physics-Fundamentals.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-event-horizon-singularity-string-theory-and-standard-model`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The terms 'Event Horizon', 'Singularity', 'String Theory' and 'Standard Model' are sometimes seen in the news in the context of

(a) Observation and understanding of the Universe  
(b) Study of the solar and the lunar eclipses  
(c) Placing satellites in the orbit of the Earth  
(d) Origin and evolution of living organisms on the Earth  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — Observation and understanding of the Universe**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The terms mentioned are fundamental concepts in modern physics and cosmology used to observe and understand the universe. 

1. **Event Horizon**: This is the 'point of no return' around a black hole. It is the boundary beyond which the gravitational pull is so strong that nothing, not even light, can escape (NASA, 'What Is a Black Hole?'). 
2. **Singularity**: In the context of general relativity, a singularity is a point in space-time where the gravitational field and density of a celestial body become infinite. This occurs at the center of a black hole and is also theorized as the starting point of the Big Bang (CERN, 'The Big Bang'). 
3. **String Theory**: This is a theoretical framework in which the point-like particles of particle physics are replaced by one-dimensional objects called strings. It is a candidate for a 'Theory of Everything' as it attempts to reconcile general relativity with quantum mechanics (NCERT Class 11 Physics, Chapter 1: Physical World). 
4. **Standard Model**: This is the theory that describes three of the four fundamental forces of nature (electromagnetic, weak, and strong interactions) and classifies all known elementary particles. It is the most robust theory in particle physics to date (CERN, 'The Standard Model'). 

Collectively, these terms are central to our scientific efforts to map, model, and comprehend the origins and mechanics of the cosmos.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Observation and understanding of the Universe** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: Study of the solar and the lunar eclipses** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Placing satellites in the orbit of the Earth** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Origin and evolution of living organisms on the Earth** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **event horizon, singularity, string theory and Standard Model**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.
