<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q045"></a>
## Q45. Genome sequencing applications in agriculture
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Genetic Engineering, GM Crops and CRISPR](../../topics/science-and-technology/genetic-engineering-gm-crops-and-crispr.md) → Genome sequencing applications in agriculture  
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Genetic Engineering, GM Crops and CRISPR → Genome sequencing applications in agriculture  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q45 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/14_Genetic-Engineering-GM-Crops-and-CRISPR.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/14_Genetic-Engineering-GM-Crops-and-CRISPR.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-genome-sequencing-applications-in-agriculture`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to agriculture in India, how can the technique of 'genome sequencing', often seen in the news, be used in the immediate future?
1. Genome sequencing can be used to identify genetic markers for disease resistance and drought tolerance in various crop plants.
2. This technique helps in reducing the time required to develop new varieties of crop plants.
3. It can be used to decipher the host-pathogen relationships in crops.

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Genome sequencing is a powerful biotechnological tool with several immediate applications in agriculture. 

1. **Identification of Genetic Markers:** By determining the entire DNA sequence of a crop, scientists can identify specific genetic markers (such as Single Nucleotide Polymorphisms or SNPs) and Quantitative Trait Loci (QTLs) that are linked to desirable traits like disease resistance and drought tolerance. This allows for 'Breeding by Design' where specific genes are targeted for improvement (Source: ICAR, PMC [6], [17]).

2. **Reducing Breeding Time:** Traditional plant breeding is a slow process that can take over a decade to stabilize a new variety. Genome sequencing enables **Marker-Assisted Selection (MAS)** and **Genomic Selection (GS)**, which allow breeders to identify whether a seedling possesses the desired traits immediately after germination. This bypasses the need to wait for the plant to reach maturity or be exposed to environmental stress, potentially reducing the breeding cycle from 10-12 years to just 3-5 years (Source: Syngenta [11], Frontiers in Plant Science [15]).

3. **Deciphering Host-Pathogen Relationships:** Sequencing the genomes of both the host plant and the attacking pathogen (fungi, bacteria, or viruses) helps researchers understand the molecular 'arms race' between them. It reveals how pathogens bypass plant immunity and how plants recognize and resist infection. This knowledge is critical for developing durable resistance in crops (Source: Nature, PMC [13], [14]).

Therefore, all three statements are correct.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q046"></a>
## Q46. Advantage of parliamentary government
**Primary route:** Polity → [Parliamentary System](../../topics/polity/prime-minister-and-council-of-ministers.md) → Advantage of parliamentary government  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Parliamentary System → Advantage of parliamentary government  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q46 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Parliamentary-System.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Parliamentary-System.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-advantage-of-parliamentary-government`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The main advantage of the parliamentary form of government is that

(a) the executive and legislature work independently.  
(b) it provides continuity of policy and is more efficient.  
(c) the executive remains responsible to the legislature.  
(d) the head of the government cannot be changed without election.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — the executive remains responsible to the legislature.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The parliamentary form of government is fundamentally characterized by the principle of 'Responsible Government.' In this system, the executive (the Council of Ministers headed by the Prime Minister) is a part of the legislature and is collectively responsible to it for its policies and actions. According to Article 75 of the Indian Constitution, the Council of Ministers is collectively responsible to the Lok Sabha (the lower house of Parliament). This ensures that the government remains in power only as long as it enjoys the confidence of the majority in the legislature. 

In contrast:
- Option 0 describes the Presidential system, where the executive and legislature are independent and operate under a strict separation of powers.
- Option 1 is often considered a demerit of the parliamentary system, as frequent changes in government or coalition instabilities can lead to a lack of policy continuity.
- Option 3 is incorrect because the head of government (Prime Minister) can be replaced by the ruling party or through a vote of no confidence without necessitating a fresh general election.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: the executive and legislature work independently.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: it provides continuity of policy and is more efficient.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: the executive remains responsible to the legislature.** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: the head of the government cannot be changed without election.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **advantage of parliamentary government**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q047"></a>
## Q47. Relationship between rights and duties
**Primary route:** Polity → [Fundamental Rights](../../topics/polity/fundamental-rights.md) → Relationship between rights and duties  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Fundamental Rights → Relationship between rights and duties  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q47 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Fundamental-Rights.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Fundamental-Rights.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-relationship-between-rights-and-duties`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In the context of India, which one of the following is the correct relationship between Rights and Duties?

(a) Rights are correlative with Duties.  
(b) Rights are personal and hence independent of society and Duties.  
(c) Rights, not Duties, are important for the advancement of the personality of the citizen.  
(d) Duties, not Rights, are important for the stability of the State.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — Rights are correlative with Duties.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

In the context of Indian political philosophy and constitutional morality, Rights and Duties are considered 'correlative' and inseparable. This means they are two sides of the same coin; the existence of a right for one individual implies a corresponding duty for others or the State to respect that right. 

1. **Philosophical Basis**: Political theorist Harold Laski argued that rights are 'conditions of social life' and are correlative with functions (duties). He stated that one man's right is also his duty to respect the similar rights of others. 
2. **Gandhian Perspective**: Mahatma Gandhi emphasized the primacy of duties, famously stating in 'Hind Swaraj' that 'Real rights are a result of the performance of duty.' He believed that if citizens perform their duties, rights would naturally follow.
3. **Constitutional Context**: While the Indian Constitution originally focused on Fundamental Rights (Part III), the 42nd Amendment Act of 1976 introduced Fundamental Duties (Part IVA) based on the Swaran Singh Committee's recommendations. This addition was intended to remind citizens that while they enjoy fundamental rights, they also owe certain duties to the nation and society. 
4. **Rejection of Other Options**: Rights are not independent of society (Option 1) because they are social claims. Both rights and duties are essential for personality development (Option 2) and state stability (Option 3); a state cannot be stable by enforcing duties alone without protecting rights, nor can a citizen's personality flourish without a sense of social responsibility (duty).

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Rights are correlative with Duties.** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: Rights are personal and hence independent of society and Duties.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Rights, not Duties, are important for the advancement of the personality of the citizen.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Duties, not Rights, are important for the stability of the State.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **relationship between rights and duties**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q048"></a>
## Q48. Preamble as key to Constitution-makers' mind
**Primary route:** Polity → [Preamble](../../topics/polity/salient-features-and-constitutional-parts.md) → Preamble as key to Constitution-makers' mind  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Preamble → Preamble as key to Constitution-makers' mind  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q48 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Preamble.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Preamble.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-preamble-as-key-to-constitution-makers-mind`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The mind of the makers of the Constitution of India is reflected in which of the following?

(a) The Preamble  
(b) The Fundamental Rights  
(c) The Directive Principles of State Policy  
(d) The Fundamental Duties  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — The Preamble**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Preamble of the Indian Constitution is considered the 'key to open the mind of the makers' of the Constitution. This specific observation was made by the Supreme Court of India in the 'Berubari Union case (1960)', where the court stated that the Preamble shows the general purposes for which the several provisions in the Constitution were made. It embodies the fundamental values—political, moral, and religious—on which the Constitution is based. It contains the noble vision of the Constituent Assembly and reflects the dreams and aspirations of the founding fathers of the Constitution. While the Preamble is not a source of substantive power, it serves as a significant guide for the interpretation of the Constitution in cases of ambiguity (M. Laxmikanth, Indian Polity, Chapter 4; NCERT Class 11, Indian Constitution at Work).

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: The Preamble** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: The Fundamental Rights** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: The Directive Principles of State Policy** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: The Fundamental Duties** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **preamble as key to Constitution-makers' mind**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

## Q49. Minimum-state route from Kohima to Kottayam
**Primary route:** Geography → [Indian Political Geography](../../topics/geography/indian-political-geography-boundaries-and-neighbours.md) → Minimum-state route from Kohima to Kottayam  
**Primary classification:** Geography → Indian Political Geography → Minimum-state route from Kohima to Kottayam

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

If you travel by road from Kohima to Kottayam, what is the minimum number of States within India through which you can travel, including the origin and the destination?

(a) 6  
(b) 7  
(c) 8  
(d) 9  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 7**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

To travel from Kohima (Nagaland) to Kottayam (Kerala) by road, the minimum number of states one must pass through is 7. The most direct route involves the following sequence: (1) Nagaland (Origin), (2) Assam (necessary to exit Nagaland towards the rest of India), (3) West Bengal (necessary to cross the Siliguri Corridor or 'Chicken's Neck'), (4) Odisha, (5) Andhra Pradesh, (6) Tamil Nadu or Karnataka (both border Kerala), and (7) Kerala (Destination). This route follows the eastern coastal corridor which minimizes the number of state borders crossed compared to inland routes through Central India. This question was part of the UPSC Civil Services Preliminary Examination 2017 (General Studies Paper I, Question 49).

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 6** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

- **C: 8** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 9** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q050"></a>
## Q50. Parliamentary control over Council of Ministers
**Primary route:** Polity → [Parliament](../../topics/polity/parliament.md) → Parliamentary control over Council of Ministers  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Parliament → Parliamentary control over Council of Ministers  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q50 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Parliament.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Parliament.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-parliamentary-control-over-council-of-ministers`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The Parliament of India exercises control over the functions of the Council of Ministers through
1. Adjournment motion
2. Question hour
3. Supplementary questions

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Parliament of India exercises control over the executive (Council of Ministers) through several procedural devices to ensure accountability and transparency. 

1. **Adjournment Motion**: This is an extraordinary device used in the Lok Sabha to draw the attention of the House to a definite matter of urgent public importance. It interrupts the regular business of the House and involves an element of censure against the government. It requires the support of at least 50 members for admission (M. Laxmikanth, Indian Polity, Chapter: Parliament).

2. **Question Hour**: The first hour of every parliamentary sitting is dedicated to Question Hour. During this time, Members of Parliament (MPs) ask questions to ministers regarding government policies and administrative activities. This is a fundamental tool for legislative oversight (NCERT Class 11, Indian Constitution at Work, Chapter 5: Legislature).

3. **Supplementary Questions**: During Question Hour, if a 'starred question' (which requires an oral answer) is asked, members are permitted to ask supplementary questions to seek further clarification or probe deeper into the minister's response. This ensures that the executive cannot evade detailed scrutiny (M. Laxmikanth, Indian Polity, Chapter: Parliament).

Since all three mechanisms are valid tools of parliamentary control, the correct answer is 1, 2, and 3.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.

## Q51. Motupalli port under the Kakatiyas
**Primary route:** Ancient History → [Peninsular India and Deccan Kingdoms](../../topics/ancient-history/peninsular-india-pallavas-chalukyas.md) → Motupalli port under the Kakatiyas  
**Primary classification:** Ancient History → Peninsular India and Deccan Kingdoms → Motupalli port under the Kakatiyas  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q51 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Ancient-Indian-History/basic/23_Peninsular-India-Pallavas-Chalukyas.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Ancient-Indian-History/basic/23_Peninsular-India-Pallavas-Chalukyas.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `ancient-history-motupalli-port-under-the-kakatiyas`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following was a very important seaport in the Kakatiya kingdom?

(a) Kakinada  
(b) Motupalli  
(c) Machilipatnam (Masulipatnam)  
(d) Nelluru  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Motupalli**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Motupalli was the most significant seaport of the Kakatiya kingdom, located in the present-day Bapatla district of Andhra Pradesh. Its importance is highlighted by the 'Abhaya Shasanam' (Charter of Security) issued by the Kakatiya ruler Ganapati Deva (c. 1199–1262 CE). This charter was an edict inscribed on a pillar to reassure foreign merchants of their safety and to regulate customs duties, thereby promoting international maritime trade. The Venetian traveler Marco Polo visited Motupalli in 1293 AD during the reign of Queen Rudrama Devi. In his travelogue, he referred to the kingdom as 'Mutfilli' and wrote extensively about its prosperity, specifically mentioning the export of high-quality diamonds and delicate muslin (buckrams). While Machilipatnam (Masulipatnam) became a major port later under the Qutb Shahi dynasty and European powers, and Nelluru (Krishnapatnam) was significant during the Vijayanagara period, Motupalli remained the primary trade hub for the Kakatiyas.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Kakinada** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: Motupalli** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: Machilipatnam (Masulipatnam)** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Nelluru** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **motupalli port under the Kakatiyas**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

## Q52. Global Climate Change Alliance
**Primary route:** Environment and Ecology → [Climate Change Science](../../topics/environment/climate-change-science-greenhouse-effect.md) → Global Climate Change Alliance  
**Primary classification:** Environment and Ecology → Climate Change Science → Global Climate Change Alliance  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q52 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/17_Climate-Change-Science-Greenhouse-Effect.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/17_Climate-Change-Science-Greenhouse-Effect.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-and-ecology-global-climate-change-alliance`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to 'Global Climate Change Alliance', which of the following statements is/are correct?
1. It is an initiative of the European Union.
2. It provides technical and financial support to targeted developing countries to integrate climate change into their development policies and budgets.
3. It is coordinated by World Resources Institute (WRI) and World Business Council for Sustainable Development (WBCSD).

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 3 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 and 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Global Climate Change Alliance (GCCA) was established by the European Union (EU) in 2007 to strengthen dialogue and cooperation with developing countries, particularly Least Developed Countries (LDCs) and Small Island Developing States (SIDS), which are most vulnerable to climate change (Statement 1 is correct). The initiative provides technical and financial support to these targeted developing countries to help them integrate climate change into their development policies and budgets, as well as to implement adaptation and mitigation projects (Statement 2 is correct). The GCCA is coordinated and managed by the European Commission (EC), not by the World Resources Institute (WRI) or the World Business Council for Sustainable Development (WBCSD). WRI and WBCSD are the organizations behind the Greenhouse Gas Protocol (GHG Protocol), which is a separate initiative for accounting and reporting greenhouse gas emissions (Statement 3 is incorrect). In 2015, the initiative entered a new phase known as the Global Climate Change Alliance Plus (GCCA+).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to the religious history of India, consider the following statements:
1. Sautrantika and Sammitiya were the sects of Jainism.
2. Sarvastivadin held that the constituents of phenomena were not wholly momentary, but existed forever in a latent form.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect because Sautrantika and Sammitiya were sects of Buddhism, not Jainism. The Sautrantika school was an early Buddhist school that emerged as an offshoot of the Sarvastivada school; they were known for their reliance on the Sutras (discourses of the Buddha) rather than the Abhidharma. The Sammitiya (also known as Pudgalavadins) were a prominent Buddhist sect that held a unique view regarding the existence of a 'person' (pudgala) as a real entity, which distinguished them from other Buddhist schools that strictly adhered to the doctrine of 'Anatta' (non-self). Statement 2 is correct. The Sarvastivada school (from the Sanskrit 'sarvam asti', meaning 'all exists') held the philosophical position that all dharmas (the fundamental constituents of phenomena) exist in the past, present, and future. While they agreed with the general Buddhist principle of momentariness regarding the manifestation of phenomena, they argued that the underlying substance (svabhava) of these constituents exists eternally in a latent or subtle form across all three periods of time. This doctrine was a major point of contention with other schools like the Theravadins, who emphasized the absolute momentariness of all conditioned things.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **sautrantika, Sammitiya and Buddhist schools**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q054"></a>
## Q54. Mediterranean-bordering countries
**Primary route:** Geography → [World Regional Geography](../../topics/geography/world-regional-geography-continents-countries.md) → Mediterranean-bordering countries  
**Primary classification:** Geography → World Regional Geography → Mediterranean-bordering countries  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q54 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/34_World-Regional-Geography-Continents-Countries.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/34_World-Regional-Geography-Continents-Countries.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `geography-mediterranean-bordering-countries`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Mediterranean Sea is a border of which of the following countries?
1. Jordan
2. Iraq
3. Lebanon
4. Syria

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1, 2 and 3 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 3 and 4 only  
(d) 1, 3 and 4 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 3 and 4 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Mediterranean Sea is an intercontinental sea bordered by 21 sovereign states across Europe, Africa, and Asia. Among the countries listed in the question:
1. **Jordan**: It is primarily a landlocked country in the Middle East. While it has a small coastline (about 26 km) on the Gulf of Aqaba, this provides access to the **Red Sea**, not the Mediterranean Sea (Britannica, Jordan; Wikipedia, Geography of Jordan).
2. **Iraq**: It is located in Western Asia and is almost entirely landlocked. Its only maritime access is a narrow coastline on the **Persian Gulf** at the tip of the Al-Faw Peninsula (Britannica, Iraq; Wikipedia, Geography of Iraq).
3. **Lebanon**: It is situated on the eastern shore of the Mediterranean Sea, which forms its entire western border (Britannica, Lebanon; Wikipedia, Geography of Lebanon).
4. **Syria**: It is located north of Lebanon and also borders the Mediterranean Sea to the west, with major port cities like Latakia and Tartus (Britannica, Syria; Wikipedia, Geography of Syria).

Therefore, only Lebanon (3) and Syria (4) border the Mediterranean Sea. This makes Option 2 (3 and 4 only) the correct answer.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **4: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1, 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 3 and 4 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: 1, 3 and 4 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

## Q55. National Investment and Infrastructure Fund
**Primary route:** Economy → [Infrastructure, PPPs, Logistics and Public Investment](../../topics/economy/infrastructure-ppps-logistics-and-public-investment.md) → National Investment and Infrastructure Fund  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Infrastructure, PPPs, Logistics and Public Investment → National Investment and Infrastructure Fund  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q55 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/18_Infrastructure-PPPs-Logistics-and-Public-Investment.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/18_Infrastructure-PPPs-Logistics-and-Public-Investment.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to 'National Investment and Infrastructure Fund', which of the following statements is/are correct?
1. It is an organ of NITI Aayog.
2. It has a corpus of ₹ 4,00,000 crore at present.

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Neither 1 nor 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect: The National Investment and Infrastructure Fund (NIIF) is not an organ of NITI Aayog. It was established by the Government of India as an investment vehicle for funding commercially viable infrastructure projects. It is registered with the Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI) as a Category II Alternative Investment Fund (AIF) and is managed by NIIF Limited, a professional asset management company (Source: NIIF Official Website, PIB). Statement 2 is incorrect: At the time of its inception and the 2017 UPSC exam, the proposed corpus of NIIF was ₹ 40,000 crore, not ₹ 4,00,000 crore. The Government of India contributes 49% of the corpus, while the remaining 51% is raised from domestic and international institutional investors such as sovereign wealth funds and pension funds (Source: PIB Release ID 133974). Therefore, neither statement is correct.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q056"></a>
## Q56. Global Infrastructure Facility
**Primary route:** Economy → [Infrastructure, PPPs, Logistics and Public Investment](../../topics/economy/infrastructure-ppps-logistics-and-public-investment.md) → Global Infrastructure Facility  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Infrastructure, PPPs, Logistics and Public Investment → Global Infrastructure Facility  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q56 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/18_Infrastructure-PPPs-Logistics-and-Public-Investment.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/18_Infrastructure-PPPs-Logistics-and-Public-Investment.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-global-infrastructure-facility`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The Global Infrastructure Facility is a/an

(a) ASEAN initiative to upgrade infrastructure in Asia and financed by credit from the Asian Development Bank.  
(b) World Bank collaboration that facilitates the preparation and structuring of complex infrastructure Public-Private Partnerships (PPPs) to enable mobilization of private sector and institutional investor capital.  
(c) Collaboration among the major banks of the world working with the OECD and focused on expanding the set of infrastructure projects that have the potential to mobilize private investment.  
(d) UNCTAD funded initiative that seeks to finance and facilitate infrastructure development in the world.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — World Bank collaboration that facilitates the preparation and structuring of complex infrastructure Public-Private Partnerships (PPPs) to enable mobilization of private sector and institutional investor capital.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Global Infrastructure Facility (GIF) is a global collaboration platform hosted by the World Bank. It was launched in 2014 to address the massive infrastructure gap in emerging markets and developing economies. The primary objective of the GIF is to facilitate the preparation and structuring of complex infrastructure Public-Private Partnerships (PPPs) that are bankable and capable of mobilizing private sector and institutional investor capital. It brings together governments, multilateral development banks (MDBs), and private sector financiers to ensure that infrastructure projects are well-designed and sustainable (World Bank, 2024). Unlike the ASEAN Infrastructure Fund or OECD-led initiatives, the GIF is specifically a World Bank-hosted partnership designed to bridge the gap between project preparation and private investment.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: ASEAN initiative to upgrade infrastructure in Asia and financed by credit from the Asian Development Bank.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: World Bank collaboration that facilitates the preparation and structuring of complex infrastructure Public-Private Partnerships (PPPs) to enable mobilization of private sector and institutional investor capital.** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: Collaboration among the major banks of the world working with the OECD and focused on expanding the set of infrastructure projects that have the potential to mobilize private investment.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: UNCTAD funded initiative that seeks to finance and facilitate infrastructure development in the world.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **global Infrastructure Facility**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q057"></a>
## Q57. Eligibility to file Lok Sabha nomination
**Primary route:** Polity → [Election Commission and Electoral Law](../../topics/polity/election-commission.md) → Eligibility to file Lok Sabha nomination  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Election Commission and Electoral Law → Eligibility to file Lok Sabha nomination  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q57 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Election-Commission.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Election-Commission.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-eligibility-to-file-lok-sabha-nomination`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

For election to the Lok Sabha, a nomination paper can be filed by

(a) anyone residing in India.  
(b) a resident of the constituency from which the election is to be contested.  
(c) any citizen of India whose name appears in the electoral roll of a constituency.  
(d) any citizen of India.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — any citizen of India whose name appears in the electoral roll of a constituency.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

According to Article 84 of the Constitution of India, a person must be a citizen of India and at least 25 years of age to be eligible for election to the Lok Sabha. Furthermore, Section 4 of the Representation of the People Act (RPA), 1951, mandates that for a general seat in the Lok Sabha, the candidate must be an 'elector for any parliamentary constituency' in India. An 'elector' is defined under Section 2(e) of the RPA 1951 as a person whose name is entered in the electoral roll of a constituency. Crucially, a candidate does not need to be a resident or a voter of the specific constituency from which they are contesting; they only need to be registered as a voter in any parliamentary constituency within the country (with specific exceptions for certain seats in states like Sikkim or the UT of Lakshadweep). Thus, any citizen of India whose name appears in the electoral roll of any constituency is eligible to file a nomination paper.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: anyone residing in India.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: a resident of the constituency from which the election is to be contested.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: any citizen of India whose name appears in the electoral roll of a constituency.** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: any citizen of India.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **eligibility to file Lok Sabha nomination**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

## Q58. Himalayas, Western Ghats and Pulicat Lake distribution
**Primary route:** Geography → [Contemporary Geographical Issues: India](../../topics/geography/indian-political-geography-boundaries-and-neighbours.md) → Himalayas, Western Ghats and Pulicat Lake distribution  
**Primary classification:** Geography → Contemporary Geographical Issues: India → Himalayas, Western Ghats and Pulicat Lake distribution  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q58 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/36_Contemporary-Geographical-Issues-India.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/36_Contemporary-Geographical-Issues-India.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `geography-himalayas-western-ghats-and-pulicat-lake-distribution`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. In India, the Himalayas are spread over five States only.
2. Western Ghats are spread over five States only.
3. Pulicat Lake is spread over two States only.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 3 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1 and 3 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The question tests the geographical distribution of major physical features in India. 

1. **Statement 1 is incorrect**: The Indian Himalayan Region (IHR) is spread over more than five states. It encompasses 11 states (Himachal Pradesh, Uttarakhand, Sikkim, Arunachal Pradesh, Nagaland, Manipur, Mizoram, Tripura, Meghalaya, and parts of Assam and West Bengal) and 2 Union Territories (Jammu & Kashmir and Ladakh). Even excluding the Union Territories, the number of states exceeds five. 

2. **Statement 2 is incorrect**: The Western Ghats are spread over six states, not five. These states are Gujarat, Maharashtra, Goa, Karnataka, Kerala, and Tamil Nadu. The range starts near the border of Gujarat and Maharashtra and runs approximately 1,600 km down to the southern tip of India. 

3. **Statement 3 is correct**: Pulicat Lake is the second largest brackish water lagoon in India (after Chilika Lake). It is situated on the Coromandel Coast and is shared by only two states: Andhra Pradesh (Nellore district) and Tamil Nadu (Thiruvallur district). 

Therefore, only statement 3 is correct, making Option 1 (3 only) the right choice.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 3 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **himalayas, Western Ghats and Pulicat Lake distribution**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q059"></a>
## Q59. Biological Oxygen Demand
**Primary route:** Environment and Ecology → [Water Pollution and River Cleaning](../../topics/environment/water-pollution-and-river-cleaning-missions.md) → Biological Oxygen Demand  
**Primary classification:** Environment and Ecology → Water Pollution and River Cleaning → Biological Oxygen Demand  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q59 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/14_Water-Pollution-and-River-Cleaning-Missions.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/14_Water-Pollution-and-River-Cleaning-Missions.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-and-ecology-biological-oxygen-demand`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Biological Oxygen Demand (BOD) is a standard criterion for

(a) Measuring oxygen levels in blood  
(b) Computing oxygen levels in forest ecosystems  
(c) Pollution assay in aquatic ecosystems  
(d) Assessing oxygen levels in high altitude regions  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Pollution assay in aquatic ecosystems**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Biological Oxygen Demand (BOD) is the amount of dissolved oxygen needed by aerobic biological organisms to break down organic material present in a given water sample at a specific temperature over a specific time period. It is a standard criterion for pollution assay in aquatic ecosystems because it indicates the level of organic pollution in the water. A high BOD value suggests that there is a large amount of organic matter being decomposed by bacteria, which consumes the dissolved oxygen available for fish and other aquatic life, potentially leading to hypoxia (NCERT Class 12 Biology, Chapter 16: Environmental Issues). Conversely, clean water has a BOD value of less than 5 ppm, while highly polluted river water can have BOD values of 17 ppm or more (NCERT Class 11 Chemistry, Chapter 14: Environmental Chemistry). The Central Pollution Control Board (CPCB) uses BOD as a primary parameter to monitor water quality and the health of aquatic ecosystems in India.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Measuring oxygen levels in blood** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: Computing oxygen levels in forest ecosystems** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Pollution assay in aquatic ecosystems** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: Assessing oxygen levels in high altitude regions** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **biological Oxygen Demand**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

## Q60. UN-Habitat's role and reporting
**Primary route:** International Relations → [UN and International Institutions](../../topics/international-relations/un-and-international-institutions-global-governance.md) → UN-Habitat's role and reporting  
**Primary classification:** International Relations → UN and International Institutions → UN-Habitat's role and reporting  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q60 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [International-Relations/basic/12_UN-and-International-Institutions-Global-Governance.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/International-Relations/basic/12_UN-and-International-Institutions-Global-Governance.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `international-relations-un-habitat-s-role-and-reporting`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to the role of UN-Habitat in the United Nations programme working towards a better urban future, which of the statements is/are correct?
1. UN-Habitat has been mandated by the United Nations General Assembly to promote socially and environmentally sustainable towns and cities to provide adequate shelter for all.
2. Its partners are either governments or local urban authorities only.
3. UN-Habitat contributes to the overall objective of the United Nations system to reduce poverty and to promote access to safe drinking water and basic sanitation.

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1, 2 and 3  
(b) 1 and 3 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 1 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct: UN-Habitat (United Nations Human Settlements Programme) is the United Nations agency for human settlements. It is mandated by the UN General Assembly (UNGA) to promote socially and environmentally sustainable towns and cities with the goal of providing adequate shelter for all (UN-Habitat Mandate). Statement 2 is incorrect: UN-Habitat works with a broad range of partners beyond just governments and local authorities. Its partners include civil society organizations, the private sector, academic institutions, foundations, and other UN agencies (UN-Habitat Partners). The use of the word 'only' in the statement makes it restrictive and factually wrong. Statement 3 is correct: UN-Habitat's work is deeply integrated with the United Nations' broader goals, specifically the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs). It contributes to poverty reduction (SDG 1) and has dedicated programs for improving access to safe drinking water and basic sanitation (SDG 6), particularly through its Urban Basic Services Branch and Water and Sanitation (WATSAN) programs (UN-Habitat Water and Sanitation).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 1 and 3 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q061"></a>
## Q61. National Skills Qualification Framework
**Primary route:** Economy → [Employment, Labour Codes, Skills and Demographic Dividend](../../topics/economy/employment-labour-codes-skills-and-demographic-dividend.md) → National Skills Qualification Framework  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Employment, Labour Codes, Skills and Demographic Dividend → National Skills Qualification Framework  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q61 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/22_Employment-Labour-Codes-Skills-and-Demographic-Dividend.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/22_Employment-Labour-Codes-Skills-and-Demographic-Dividend.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-national-skills-qualification-framework`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to 'National Skills Qualification Framework (NSQF)', which of the statements given below is/are correct?
1. Under NSQF, a learner can acquire the certification for competency only through formal learning.
2. An outcome expected from the implementation of NSQF is the mobility between vocational and general education.

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The National Skills Qualification Framework (NSQF), notified in 2013, is a competency-based framework that organizes qualifications according to a series of levels of knowledge, skills, and aptitude. 

1. **Statement 1 is incorrect**: The NSQF explicitly allows for the acquisition of certification through formal, non-formal, and informal learning. A key component of the NSQF is the **Recognition of Prior Learning (RPL)**, which enables individuals with existing skills (acquired through work experience or informal means) to be assessed and certified. According to the Ministry of Skill Development and Entrepreneurship (MSDE), the framework is designed to accommodate learning outcomes regardless of the mode of acquisition (PIB, 2024; MSDE NSQF Gazette Notification).

2. **Statement 2 is correct**: One of the primary objectives of the NSQF is to provide a bridge between vocational and general education. It facilitates **horizontal and vertical mobility** by establishing academic equivalence and a credit-based system, allowing students to move between vocational training, technical education, and general academic streams (MSDE, 2023; NCVET). This helps in removing the 'silo' approach in the Indian education system and provides multiple entry and exit points.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **national Skills Qualification Framework**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q062"></a>
## Q62. Meaning of dyarchy
**Primary route:** Modern History → [Colonial Government Structure and Constitutional Development](../../topics/modern-history/colonial-government-structure-and-constitutional-development.md) → Meaning of dyarchy  
**Primary classification:** Modern History → Colonial Government Structure and Constitutional Development → Meaning of dyarchy  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q62 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/06_Government-Structure-and-Constitutional-Development-1757-1858.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/06_Government-Structure-and-Constitutional-Development-1757-1858.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-meaning-of-dyarchy`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In the context of Indian history, the principle of 'Dyarchy (diarchy)' refers to

(a) Division of the central legislature into two houses.  
(b) Introduction of double government i.e., Central and State governments.  
(c) Having two sets of rulers; one in London and another in Delhi.  
(d) Division of the subjects delegated to the provinces into two categories.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Division of the subjects delegated to the provinces into two categories.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

In the context of Indian history, the principle of 'Dyarchy' (derived from the Greek word 'di-arche' meaning double rule) refers to the system of double government introduced at the provincial level by the Government of India Act of 1919, also known as the Montagu-Chelmsford Reforms. Under this system, the provincial subjects of administration were divided into two distinct categories: 'Reserved' and 'Transferred'. The 'Reserved' subjects, which included critical areas like law and order, finance, land revenue, and justice, were administered by the Governor and his executive council without any responsibility to the provincial legislature. The 'Transferred' subjects, such as education, public health, local self-government, and agriculture, were administered by the Governor acting with the advice of ministers who were responsible to the Legislative Council (Laxmikanth, Indian Polity, Chapter 1; NCERT Class 12, Themes in Indian History Part III). This system aimed to introduce a degree of representative government while maintaining British control over vital administrative functions. It was eventually replaced by 'Provincial Autonomy' under the Government of India Act of 1935.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Division of the central legislature into two houses.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: Introduction of double government i.e., Central and State governments.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Having two sets of rulers; one in London and another in Delhi.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Division of the subjects delegated to the provinces into two categories.** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **meaning of dyarchy**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following in respect of 'National Career Service':
1. National Career Service is an initiative of the Department of Personnel and Training, Government of India.
2. National Career Service has been launched in a Mission Mode to improve the employment opportunities to uneducated youth of the country.

Which of the above statements is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Neither 1 nor 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect: The National Career Service (NCS) is an initiative of the Ministry of Labour and Employment (MoLE), Government of India, not the Department of Personnel and Training (DoPT). It is implemented by the Directorate General of Employment (DGE) within the Ministry of Labour and Employment. Statement 2 is incorrect: While the National Career Service was indeed launched as a 'Mission Mode Project' (on July 20, 2015), its objective is to provide a comprehensive range of employment and career-related services to all citizens, including educated, uneducated, skilled, and unskilled job seekers. It is not exclusively targeted at 'uneducated youth' but serves as a one-stop solution for job seekers, employers, career counselors, and training providers to bridge the gap in the employment market. It offers services like dynamic job matching, career counseling, vocational guidance, and information on skill development and internships.

- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q064"></a>
## Q64. S4A stressed-assets scheme
**Primary route:** Economy → [NPAs, Resolution and Financial Inclusion](../../topics/economy/banking-structure-nbfcs-and-financial-regulation.md) → S4A stressed-assets scheme  
**Primary classification:** Economy → NPAs, Resolution and Financial Inclusion → S4A stressed-assets scheme  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q64 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/06_NPAs-Basel-Norms-Resolution-and-Financial-Inclusion.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/06_NPAs-Basel-Norms-Resolution-and-Financial-Inclusion.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-s4a-stressed-assets-scheme`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following statements best describes the term 'Scheme for Sustainable Structuring of Stressed Assets (S4A)', recently seen in the news?

(a) It is a procedure for considering ecological costs of developmental schemes formulated by the Government.  
(b) It is a scheme of RBI for reworking the financial structure of big corporate entities facing genuine difficulties.  
(c) It is a disinvestment plan of the Government regarding Central Public Sector Undertakings.  
(d) It is an important provision in 'The Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code' recently implemented by the Government.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — It is a scheme of RBI for reworking the financial structure of big corporate entities facing genuine difficulties.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Scheme for Sustainable Structuring of Stressed Assets (S4A) was introduced by the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) on June 13, 2016, as a mechanism to address the rising problem of Non-Performing Assets (NPAs) in the Indian banking sector. The scheme was specifically designed for large corporate entities where the total exposure of all institutional lenders was more than ₹500 crore and the project had already commenced commercial operations. Under S4A, the debt of a stressed company is divided into two parts: 'Sustainable Debt' (the portion that can be serviced by the company's current cash flows) and 'Unsustainable Debt' (the remaining portion, which is converted into equity or quasi-equity instruments like optionally convertible debentures). This allows the company to continue its operations without the immediate burden of unserviceable debt while providing banks with a potential upside if the company recovers. Unlike the Strategic Debt Restructuring (SDR) scheme, S4A allowed the existing management to continue as long as there was no evidence of wilful default. It was one of several pre-IBC (Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code) tools used by the RBI to manage stressed assets.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: It is a procedure for considering ecological costs of developmental schemes formulated by the Government.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: It is a scheme of RBI for reworking the financial structure of big corporate entities facing genuine difficulties.** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: It is a disinvestment plan of the Government regarding Central Public Sector Undertakings.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: It is an important provision in 'The Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code' recently implemented by the Government.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **s4A stressed-assets scheme**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. Climate and Clean Air Coalition (CCAC) to Reduce Short Lived Climate Pollutants is a unique initiative of G20 group of countries.
2. The CCAC focuses on methane, black carbon and hydrofluorocarbons.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect because the Climate and Clean Air Coalition (CCAC) is not an initiative of the G20. It was launched in February 2012 by the United Nations Environment Programme (UNEP) and six founding countries: Bangladesh, Canada, Ghana, Mexico, Sweden, and the United States. It is a voluntary partnership of governments, intergovernmental organizations, and non-governmental organizations. Statement 2 is correct as the CCAC specifically targets 'Short-Lived Climate Pollutants' (SLCPs), which include methane, black carbon, and hydrofluorocarbons (HFCs), along with tropospheric ozone. These pollutants have a relatively short atmospheric lifetime but a high global warming potential compared to carbon dioxide.

- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q066"></a>
## Q66. Indian Ocean Dipole and monsoon
**Primary route:** Geography → [Tropical Monsoon and Marine Climate](../../topics/geography/tropical-monsoon-and-marine-climate.md) → Indian Ocean Dipole and monsoon  
**Primary classification:** Geography → Tropical Monsoon and Marine Climate → Indian Ocean Dipole and monsoon  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q66 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/16_Tropical-Monsoon-and-Marine-Climate.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/16_Tropical-Monsoon-and-Marine-Climate.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `geography-indian-ocean-dipole-and-monsoon`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to 'Indian Ocean Dipole (IOD)' sometimes mentioned in the news while forecasting Indian monsoon, which of the following statements is/are correct?
1. IOD phenomenon is characterised by a difference in sea surface temperature between tropical Western Indian Ocean and tropical Eastern Pacific Ocean.
2. An IOD phenomenon can influence an El Niño's impact on the monsoon.

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Indian Ocean Dipole (IOD) is an ocean-atmosphere phenomenon in the tropical Indian Ocean. 

1. **Statement 1 is incorrect**: The IOD is characterized by a difference in sea surface temperature (SST) between the **tropical Western Indian Ocean** (Arabian Sea) and the **tropical Eastern Indian Ocean** (south of Indonesia). The statement incorrectly identifies the second pole as being in the 'Eastern Pacific Ocean', which is actually the region associated with the El Niño-Southern Oscillation (ENSO) rather than the IOD (Source: India Meteorological Department, Edukemy UPSC PYQ Analysis).

2. **Statement 2 is correct**: The IOD can significantly influence the impact of El Niño on the Indian monsoon. While El Niño generally has a drying effect on the Indian monsoon, a **positive IOD** (warmer western Indian Ocean) can mitigate this effect by bringing more moisture and rainfall to the Indian subcontinent. For instance, in 1997, a strong positive IOD offset a powerful El Niño, resulting in normal monsoon rainfall in India (Source: The Indian Express, Drishti IAS). 

Therefore, only statement 2 is correct.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **indian Ocean Dipole and monsoon**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

If you want to see gharials in their natural habitat, which one of the following is the best place to visit?

(a) Bhitarkanika Mangroves  
(b) Chambal River  
(c) Pulicat Lake  
(d) Deepor Beel  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Chambal River**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The National Chambal Sanctuary, located on the Chambal River at the tri-junction of Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh, and Uttar Pradesh, is the best place to see gharials (Gavialis gangeticus) in their natural habitat. It is a dedicated sanctuary for this critically endangered species and currently holds approximately 80% of the global wild gharial population (Source: Wildlife Institute of India). 

In contrast, the other options are known for different wildlife:
- **Bhitarkanika Mangroves (Odisha):** This site is famous for its large population of Saltwater Crocodiles (Crocodylus porosus) and is a Ramsar wetland.
- **Pulicat Lake (Andhra Pradesh/Tamil Nadu):** This is a brackish water lagoon primarily known for its avian diversity, particularly the Greater Flamingo.
- **Deepor Beel (Assam):** A permanent freshwater lake and Ramsar site known for its rich birdlife and as a corridor for wild Asiatic elephants.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Bhitarkanika Mangroves** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: Chambal River** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.

- **D: Deepor Beel** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect because the inaugural Indian Ocean Naval Symposium (IONS) was held in February 2008 in New Delhi, India, not in 2015. The Indian Navy hosted the first seminar and chaired the symposium from 2008 to 2010 (Source: Indian Navy Official Website, PIB). Statement 2 is correct as IONS is a voluntary initiative that seeks to increase maritime cooperation among navies of the littoral states of the Indian Ocean Region (IOR). It provides an open and inclusive forum for discussion of regionally relevant maritime issues, such as maritime security, humanitarian assistance, and disaster relief (Source: IONS Official Website, PIB).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
