<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q069"></a>
## Q69. Bodhisattva Padmapani painting
**Primary route:** Art and Culture → [Painting Traditions](../../topics/art-and-culture/painting-traditions.md) → Bodhisattva Padmapani painting  
**Primary classification:** Art and Culture → Painting Traditions → Bodhisattva Padmapani painting  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q69 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Indian-Art-and-Culture/basic/07_Painting-Traditions.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Indian-Art-and-Culture/basic/07_Painting-Traditions.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `art-and-culture-bodhisattva-padmapani-painting`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The painting of Bodhisattva Padmapani is one of the most famous and oft-illustrated paintings at

(a) Ajanta  
(b) Badami  
(c) Bagh  
(d) Ellora  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — Ajanta**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The painting of Bodhisattva Padmapani is located in Cave 1 of the Ajanta Caves in Maharashtra, India. It is one of the most iconic and celebrated examples of ancient Indian mural art, dating back to the late 5th century CE during the Vakataka-Gupta period (Britannica). The term 'Padmapani' literally means 'the one who holds a lotus in his hand' (Padma = lotus, Pani = hand). In this painting, the Bodhisattva is depicted in a graceful 'tribhanga' (triple-bend) pose, holding a blue lotus, with a serene and compassionate expression (NCERT Class 11). This artwork is renowned for its sophisticated use of color, light, and shade, reflecting the pinnacle of Buddhist art at Ajanta. While other sites like Ellora, Badami, and Bagh also contain significant rock-cut architecture and paintings, the specific 'Bodhisattva Padmapani' mural is uniquely associated with Ajanta Cave 1 (UNESCO).

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Ajanta** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: Badami** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Bagh** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Ellora** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q070"></a>
## Q70. Religious festivals and communities
**Primary route:** Art and Culture → [Crafts, Folk and Tribal Traditions](../../topics/art-and-culture/crafts-textiles-folk-and-tribal-traditions.md) → Religious festivals and communities  
**Primary classification:** Art and Culture → Crafts, Folk and Tribal Traditions → Religious festivals and communities

**Canonical owner:** [Indian-Art-and-Culture/basic/12_Crafts-Textiles-Folk-and-Tribal-Traditions.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Indian-Art-and-Culture/basic/12_Crafts-Textiles-Folk-and-Tribal-Traditions.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `art-and-culture-religious-festivals-and-communities`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following pairs:
Traditions — Communities
1. Chaliha Sahib Festival — Sindhis
2. Nanda Raj Jaat Yatra — Gonds
3. Wari-Warkari — Santhals

Which of the pairs given above is/are correctly matched?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) None of the above  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The pairs are evaluated as follows: 
1. **Chaliha Sahib Festival — Sindhis**: This is correctly matched. Chaliha Sahib is a 40-day fast observed by the Sindhi community to express gratitude to Jhulelal (Uderolal), who is believed to have saved them from the religious persecution of the ruler Mirkshah (Source: PIB, 'Sindhi Community celebrates Chaliha Sahib').
2. **Nanda Raj Jaat Yatra — Gonds**: This is incorrectly matched. The Nanda Raj Jaat Yatra is a major pilgrimage and festival in Uttarakhand, dedicated to Goddess Nanda Devi. It is primarily observed by the people of the Garhwal and Kumaon regions, whereas the Gonds are a tribal community primarily residing in Central India (Source: Uttarakhand Tourism Development Board).
3. **Wari-Warkari — Santhals**: This is incorrectly matched. Wari (or Vari) is an annual pilgrimage to Pandharpur, Maharashtra, to honor Lord Vithoba. It is a tradition of the Warkari sect in Maharashtra, which follows the teachings of Bhakti saints like Dnyaneshwar and Tukaram. The Santhals are a tribal group mainly found in Jharkhand, West Bengal, and Odisha (Source: NCERT Class 12 History, Themes in Indian History Part II, Chapter 6: Bhakti-Sufi Traditions).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: None of the above** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q071"></a>
## Q71. Water-conservation practices in agriculture
**Primary route:** Economy → [Irrigation, Inputs and Sustainable Agriculture](../../topics/economy/irrigation-inputs-credit-insurance-and-sustainable-agriculture.md) → Water-conservation practices in agriculture  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Irrigation, Inputs and Sustainable Agriculture → Water-conservation practices in agriculture  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q71 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/14_Irrigation-Inputs-Credit-Insurance-and-Sustainable-Agriculture.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/14_Irrigation-Inputs-Credit-Insurance-and-Sustainable-Agriculture.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-water-conservation-practices-in-agriculture`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following practices can help in water conservation in agriculture?
1. Reduced or zero tillage of the land
2. Applying gypsum before irrigating the field
3. Allowing crop residue to remain in the field

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Reduced or zero tillage conserves soil moisture by limiting disturbance, retaining residues and improving infiltration; statement 1 is correct. Applying gypsum is primarily a soil-amendment measure for sodic soils and is not, by itself, a general water-conservation practice; statement 2 is incorrect. Retaining crop residue reduces evaporation, moderates soil temperature and improves infiltration; statement 3 is correct. Therefore the official-final answer is 1 and 3 only.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q072"></a>
## Q72. Soil Health Card Scheme
**Primary route:** Economy → [Irrigation, Inputs and Sustainable Agriculture](../../topics/economy/irrigation-inputs-credit-insurance-and-sustainable-agriculture.md) → Soil Health Card Scheme  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Irrigation, Inputs and Sustainable Agriculture → Soil Health Card Scheme  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q72 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/14_Irrigation-Inputs-Credit-Insurance-and-Sustainable-Agriculture.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/14_Irrigation-Inputs-Credit-Insurance-and-Sustainable-Agriculture.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-soil-health-card-scheme`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
The nation-wide 'Soil Health Card Scheme' aims at
1. expanding the cultivable area under irrigation.
2. enabling the banks to assess the quantum of loans to be granted to farmers on the basis of soil quality.
3. checking the overuse of fertilizers in farmlands.

Which of the above statements is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 3 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Soil Health Card (SHC) Scheme was launched by the Government of India in February 2015 with the primary objective of providing farmers with a printed report (the Soil Health Card) that assesses the nutrient status of their soil across 12 parameters (N, P, K, S, Zn, Fe, Cu, Mn, B, pH, EC, and OC). 

1. **Statement 1 is incorrect**: The scheme focuses on soil nutrient management and fertility, not on expanding irrigation infrastructure. The expansion of cultivable area under irrigation is the primary objective of the 'Pradhan Mantri Krishi Sinchayee Yojana' (PMKSY).
2. **Statement 2 is incorrect**: There is no provision in the SHC scheme that mandates or enables banks to use soil quality data to determine the quantum of loans. Agricultural loans in India are generally assessed based on land records, crop type, and the 'Scale of Finance' determined by District Level Technical Committees, often facilitated through the Kisan Credit Card (KCC) scheme.
3. **Statement 3 is correct**: A core aim of the SHC scheme is to promote 'balanced fertilization' by providing crop-wise recommendations for nutrients and fertilizers. This helps farmers avoid the indiscriminate and excessive use of chemical fertilizers (like Urea), thereby reducing input costs and preventing soil degradation.

Therefore, only statement 3 is correct.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 3 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **soil Health Card Scheme**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

## Q73. Consumer products and chemical constituents
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [General Science: Chemistry Fundamentals](../../topics/science-and-technology/general-science-chemistry-fundamentals.md) → Consumer products and chemical constituents  
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → General Science: Chemistry Fundamentals → Consumer products and chemical constituents  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q73 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/22_General-Science-Chemistry-Fundamentals.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/22_General-Science-Chemistry-Fundamentals.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-consumer-products-and-chemical-constituents`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following pairs:
Commonly used / consumed materials — Unwanted or controversial chemicals likely to be found in them
1. Lipstick — Lead
2. Soft drinks — Brominated vegetable oils
3. Chinese fast food — Monosodium glutamate

Which of the pairs given above is/are correctly matched?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

All three pairs are correctly matched based on common food safety and cosmetic industry controversies:

1. **Lipstick — Lead**: Lead is a heavy metal that is often found as a trace contaminant in lipsticks, primarily originating from the mineral-based color additives used. The US Food and Drug Administration (FDA) conducted a study in 2012 that found trace amounts of lead in 400 lipsticks tested. While the FDA maintains that these levels do not pose a safety risk, lead remains a controversial 'unwanted' chemical in cosmetics due to its tendency to accumulate in the body (FDA, 'Lead in Lipstick').

2. **Soft drinks — Brominated vegetable oils (BVO)**: BVO is a food additive used as an emulsifier in citrus-flavored soft drinks to prevent the flavoring oils from separating and floating to the top. It has been controversial because bromine can accumulate in human tissues, potentially leading to neurological issues or thyroid dysfunction. BVO was banned in India in 1990 and has been phased out by major global beverage companies (FSSAI; WebMD, 'Is Brominated Vegetable Oil Safe?').

3. **Chinese fast food — Monosodium glutamate (MSG)**: MSG is a flavor enhancer that provides the 'umami' taste. It is widely associated with Chinese fast food and became controversial following reports of 'Chinese Restaurant Syndrome,' a collection of symptoms like headaches and palpitations. While regulatory bodies like the FDA and FSSAI generally recognize it as safe (GRAS) at normal levels, it remains a subject of public health debate and labeling requirements (The Guardian, 'Chinese restaurant syndrome').

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **consumer products and chemical constituents**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q074"></a>
## Q74. Advantages of OLED displays
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Semiconductors and Electronics](../../topics/science-and-technology/semiconductor-mission-and-electronics-manufacturing.md) → Advantages of OLED displays  
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Semiconductors and Electronics → Advantages of OLED displays  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q74 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/11_Semiconductor-Mission-and-Electronics-Manufacturing.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/11_Semiconductor-Mission-and-Electronics-Manufacturing.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-advantages-of-oled-displays`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Organic Light Emitting Diodes (OLEDs) are used to create digital display in many devices. What are the advantages of OLED displays over Liquid Crystal displays?
1. OLED displays can be fabricated on flexible plastic substrates.
2. Roll-up displays embedded in clothing can be made using OLEDs.
3. Transparent displays are possible using OLEDs.

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 and 3 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 1, 2 and 3  
(d) None of the above statements is correct  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Organic Light Emitting Diodes (OLEDs) offer several distinct advantages over Liquid Crystal Displays (LCDs) due to their self-emissive nature and material properties. 

1. **Flexible Plastic Substrates**: Unlike LCDs, which require a rigid glass substrate to contain liquid crystals and a backlight, OLEDs are made of thin organic films that can be deposited on flexible materials like plastic (e.g., polyimide or PET) or metal foils. This allows for the creation of curved, foldable, and bendable screens (Statement 1 is correct) [1, 4, 10]. 

2. **Roll-up Displays and Clothing**: Because OLEDs can be fabricated on extremely thin and flexible substrates, they can be engineered into rollable formats (like LG's rollable TVs) or integrated into textiles to create 'smart' clothing or wearable displays (Statement 2 is correct) [1, 6, 7]. 

3. **Transparent Displays**: OLEDs do not require a backlight. By using transparent electrodes (like Indium Tin Oxide) and transparent substrates, manufacturers can create Transparent OLEDs (TOLEDs). These displays allow light to pass through them when turned off and can show images while remaining semi-transparent when turned on (Statement 3 is correct) [1, 5, 7]. 

Since all three statements are correct, the correct option is 1, 2, and 3.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: None of the above statements is correct** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **advantages of OLED displays**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q075"></a>
## Q75. Sun temples of India
**Primary route:** Art and Culture → [Temple Architecture](../../topics/art-and-culture/temple-architecture-and-chandella-khajuraho.md) → Sun temples of India  
**Primary classification:** Art and Culture → Temple Architecture → Sun temples of India  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q75 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Indian-Art-and-Culture/basic/03_Temple-Architecture-and-Chandella-Khajuraho.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Indian-Art-and-Culture/basic/03_Temple-Architecture-and-Chandella-Khajuraho.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `art-and-culture-sun-temples-of-india`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following is/are famous for Sun temples?
1. Arasavalli
2. Amarakantak
3. Omkareshwar

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The question asks to identify locations famous for Sun temples among the given options. 

1. **Arasavalli**: Located in the Srikakulam district of Andhra Pradesh, Arasavalli is home to the **Sri Suryanarayana Swamy Temple**. This is one of the most ancient and famous Sun temples in India, believed to have been built in the 7th century CE by King Devendra Varma of the Eastern Ganga Dynasty. The temple is architecturally significant because the sun's rays fall on the feet of the deity twice a year (during Uttarayanam and Dakshinayam), even when the entrance gates are closed. Thus, statement 1 is correct.

2. **Amarakantak**: Located in Madhya Pradesh, Amarakantak is primarily famous as the **source of the Narmada and Son rivers**. It is a significant pilgrimage site known as 'Teerthraj' (the king of pilgrimages) and houses temples like the Narmada Udgam Temple and the ancient Kalachuri group of temples (dedicated to Shiva and Vishnu). It is not famous for a Sun temple. Thus, statement 2 is incorrect.

3. **Omkareshwar**: Also located in Madhya Pradesh on an island in the Narmada River, Omkareshwar is famous for the **Omkareshwar Jyotirlinga**, which is one of the twelve highly revered shrines dedicated to Lord Shiva. While the island has many temples, its primary fame is as a Shiva pilgrimage site, not a Sun temple. Thus, statement 3 is incorrect.

Therefore, only Arasavalli is famous for a Sun temple among the choices provided.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q076"></a>
## Q76. Liquefied petroleum gas properties
**Primary route:** Economy → [Energy Infrastructure and Fuels](../../topics/economy/energy-infrastructure-economics-power-fuels-and-energy-security.md) → Liquefied petroleum gas properties  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Energy Infrastructure and Fuels → Liquefied petroleum gas properties  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q76 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/31_Energy-Infrastructure-Economics-Power-Fuels-and-Energy-Security.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/31_Energy-Infrastructure-Economics-Power-Fuels-and-Energy-Security.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-liquefied-petroleum-gas-properties`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. In the election for Lok Sabha or State Assembly, the winning candidate must get at least 50 percent of the votes polled, to be declared elected.
2. According to the provisions laid down in the Constitution of India, the Speaker's post goes to the majority party and the Deputy Speaker's to the Opposition.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Neither 1 nor 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect because India follows the 'First-Past-The-Post' (FPTP) system for Lok Sabha and State Legislative Assembly elections. In this system, the candidate who secures the highest number of votes in a constituency is declared elected, regardless of whether they cross the 50% threshold (NCERT Class 11, Indian Constitution at Work, Chapter 3). Statement 2 is incorrect because the Constitution of India does not mandate that the Speaker must be from the majority party and the Deputy Speaker from the Opposition. Article 93 merely states that the House shall choose two members to be Speaker and Deputy Speaker. The practice of electing a Speaker from the ruling party and a Deputy Speaker from the main opposition is a parliamentary convention that evolved over time, specifically gaining ground since the 11th Lok Sabha in 1996 (M. Laxmikanth, Indian Polity, Chapter 22).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q077"></a>
## Q77. Post-1991 liberalisation outcomes
**Primary route:** Economy → [Industrial Policy and 1991 Reforms](../../topics/economy/industrial-policy-1991-reforms-psus-and-disinvestment.md) → Post-1991 liberalisation outcomes  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Industrial Policy and 1991 Reforms → Post-1991 liberalisation outcomes  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q77 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/16_Industrial-Policy-1991-Reforms-PSUs-and-Disinvestment.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/16_Industrial-Policy-1991-Reforms-PSUs-and-Disinvestment.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-post-1991-liberalisation-outcomes`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following has/have occurred in India after its liberalization of economic policies in 1991?
1. Share of agriculture in GDP increased enormously.
2. Share of India's exports in world trade increased.
3. FDI inflows increased.
4. India's foreign exchange reserves increased enormously.

Select the correct answer using the codes given below:

(a) 1 and 4 only  
(b) 2, 3 and 4 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2, 3 and 4 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Following the 1991 economic liberalization (LPG reforms), India's economy underwent structural shifts. 
1. **Share of agriculture in GDP:** This statement is incorrect. While agricultural production increased, its relative share in the GDP has steadily declined as the services and manufacturing sectors grew faster. In 1990-91, agriculture's share was approximately 29-30%, but it dropped to around 17-18% by 2016-17 (NCERT Class 11 Economics, Chapter 3; PIB 2011). 
2. **Share of India's exports in world trade:** This statement is correct. India's share in global merchandise exports was about 0.5% in 1991 and increased to approximately 1.7% by 2015, reflecting greater global integration (Economic Survey 2016-17; WTO Data). 
3. **FDI inflows:** This statement is correct. Liberalization removed many barriers to foreign investment. FDI inflows rose from a negligible $129 million in 1991-92 to over $60 billion in the 2016-17 fiscal year (DPIIT Statistics; RBI Handbook). 
4. **Foreign exchange reserves:** This statement is correct. In 1991, India faced a Balance of Payments crisis with reserves plummeting to about $1.2 billion (barely enough for two weeks of imports). By 2017, these reserves had increased enormously to over $370 billion (RBI Data; ClearTax).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **4: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 4 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2, 3 and 4 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 1, 2, 3 and 4** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **post-1991 liberalisation outcomes**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q078"></a>
## Q78. Somatic Cell Nuclear Transfer
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Biotechnology Fundamentals](../../topics/science-and-technology/biotechnology-fundamentals-and-dbt-missions.md) → Somatic Cell Nuclear Transfer  
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Biotechnology Fundamentals → Somatic Cell Nuclear Transfer  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q78 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/13_Biotechnology-Fundamentals-and-DBT-Missions.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/13_Biotechnology-Fundamentals-and-DBT-Missions.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-somatic-cell-nuclear-transfer`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

What is the application of Somatic Cell Nuclear Transfer Technology?

(a) Production of biolarvicides  
(b) Manufacture of biodegradable plastics  
(c) Reproductive cloning of animals  
(d) Production of organisms free of diseases  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Reproductive cloning of animals**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Somatic Cell Nuclear Transfer (SCNT) is a laboratory technique used to create a viable embryo from a body cell and an egg cell. The process involves two main steps: first, the nucleus (which contains the genetic material) is removed from a female gamete (egg cell or oocyte), a process called enucleation. Second, the nucleus of a somatic cell (any body cell other than a sperm or egg cell, such as a skin cell) is implanted into this enucleated egg. The egg is then stimulated to divide, eventually forming an embryo that is genetically identical to the donor of the somatic cell. The most famous application of this technology was the creation of 'Dolly the sheep' in 1996, the first mammal cloned from an adult somatic cell. SCNT is primarily used for 'Reproductive cloning of animals' (to create identical copies of animals) and 'Therapeutic cloning' (to generate embryonic stem cells for medical research and regenerative medicine). It is not used for producing biolarvicides or biodegradable plastics, and while it can propagate disease-free traits through cloning, its fundamental application is the cloning process itself (Source: NCERT Class 12 Biology, Chapter 11 & 12; UPSC Official Answer Key 2017).

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Production of biolarvicides** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: Manufacture of biodegradable plastics** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Reproductive cloning of animals** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: Production of organisms free of diseases** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **somatic Cell Nuclear Transfer**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

## Q79. NPCI and digital-payment systems
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Digital India and India Stack](../../topics/science-and-technology/digital-india-india-stack-and-digital-infrastructure.md) → NPCI and digital-payment systems  
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Digital India and India Stack → NPCI and digital-payment systems  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q79 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/08_Digital-India-and-India-Stack-UPI-Aadhaar.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/08_Digital-India-and-India-Stack-UPI-Aadhaar.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-npci-and-digital-payment-systems`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. National Payments Corporation of India (NPCI) helps in promoting the financial inclusion in the country.
2. NPCI has launched RuPay, a card payment scheme.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Both 1 and 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Both statements are correct. 

1. **Statement 1 is correct**: The National Payments Corporation of India (NPCI) is an umbrella organization for operating retail payments and settlement systems in India. It was established in 2008 as an initiative of the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) and the Indian Banks' Association (IBA) under the provisions of the Payment and Settlement Systems Act, 2007. NPCI plays a pivotal role in promoting financial inclusion through its various products like the Aadhaar Enabled Payment System (AePS), which allows people to access banking services using their Aadhaar and biometrics, and the Aadhaar Payment Bridge System (APBS), which facilitates Direct Benefit Transfers (DBT). These initiatives are central to the government's financial inclusion agenda (Jan Dhan-Aadhaar-Mobile trinity).

2. **Statement 2 is correct**: RuPay is an indigenous card payment scheme launched by the NPCI in 2012. It was conceived to fulfill the RBI's vision of establishing a domestic, open, and multilateral system of payments in India. RuPay cards are widely used under the Pradhan Mantri Jan Dhan Yojana (PMJDY) to provide banking access to the unbanked population, further supporting financial inclusion.

Grounding References: NPCI Official Website (About Us), RBI's Payment and Settlement Systems in India: Vision 2012-15, and PIB releases regarding the launch of RuPay and its role in PMJDY.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

## Q80. M-STrIPES tiger monitoring
**Primary route:** Environment and Ecology → [Wildlife Protection Act and Schedules](../../topics/environment/wildlife-protection-act-and-schedules.md) → M-STrIPES tiger monitoring  
**Primary classification:** Environment and Ecology → Wildlife Protection Act and Schedules → M-STrIPES tiger monitoring  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q80 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/08_Wildlife-Protection-Act-and-Schedules.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/08_Wildlife-Protection-Act-and-Schedules.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-and-ecology-m-stripes-tiger-monitoring`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The term 'M-STrIPES' is sometimes seen in the news in the context of

(a) Captive breeding of Wild Fauna  
(b) Maintenance of Tiger Reserves  
(c) Indigenous Satellite Navigation System  
(d) Security of National Highways  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Maintenance of Tiger Reserves**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

M-STrIPES, which stands for 'Monitoring System for Tigers - Intensive Protection and Ecological Status', is a software-based monitoring system launched by the National Tiger Conservation Authority (NTCA) in 2010. Its primary purpose is to strengthen the patrolling and surveillance of tiger reserves across India (Source: NTCA). The system consists of two main components: a field-based mobile application for forest guards to record data during patrols (using GPS and GPRS) and a desktop-based analysis tool for managers to process this data. It allows for the tracking of patrol efforts, recording of wildlife sightings, and monitoring of illegal activities or ecological changes, thereby facilitating the scientific maintenance and management of tiger reserves (Source: Wildlife Institute of India). By digitizing patrol records, it ensures transparency and accountability in the protection of tiger habitats (Source: PIB).

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q081"></a>
## Q81. Advantages of Goods and Services Tax
**Primary route:** Economy → [Taxation, GST and Fiscal Federalism](../../topics/economy/taxation-gst-finance-commission-and-fiscal-federalism.md) → Advantages of Goods and Services Tax  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Taxation, GST and Fiscal Federalism → Advantages of Goods and Services Tax  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q81 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/10_Taxation-GST-Finance-Commission-and-Fiscal-Federalism.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/10_Taxation-GST-Finance-Commission-and-Fiscal-Federalism.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-advantages-of-goods-and-services-tax`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

What is/are the most likely advantages of implementing 'Goods and Services Tax (GST)'?
1. It will replace multiple taxes collected by multiple authorities and will thus create a single market in India.
2. It will drastically reduce the 'Current Account Deficit' of India and will enable it to increase its foreign exchange reserves.
3. It will enormously increase the growth and size of economy of India and will enable it to overtake China in the near future.

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Goods and Services Tax (GST), implemented in India on July 1, 2017, was designed to streamline the indirect tax regime. 

1. **Statement 1 is correct**: One of the primary objectives of GST was to subsume multiple central and state indirect taxes—such as Central Excise Duty, Service Tax, Value Added Tax (VAT), and Entry Tax—into a single tax structure. This unification eliminates interstate tax barriers and the cascading effect of 'tax on tax,' thereby creating a common national market (PIB, 'An overview of GST').

2. **Statement 2 is incorrect**: While GST makes Indian exports more competitive by allowing for the zero-rating of exports and seamless input tax credits, it does not 'drastically' reduce the Current Account Deficit (CAD). The CAD is influenced by broader macroeconomic factors, including global crude oil prices, trade balances, and invisible earnings like remittances. GST is a domestic tax reform and not a direct tool for managing foreign exchange reserves or the CAD (Edukemy, 'UPSC Prelims 2017 – Question 81').

3. **Statement 3 is incorrect**: Although GST is expected to boost India's GDP growth (with various estimates suggesting an increase of 1% to 2%), the claim that it will 'enormously' increase the economy's size to 'overtake China in the near future' is an exaggeration. China's economy is significantly larger than India's, and such a transition involves complex structural factors beyond a single tax reform (SuperKalam, 'UPSC Prelims 2017 GS Answer').

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **advantages of Goods and Services Tax**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q082"></a>
## Q82. India-EU Broad-based Trade and Investment Agreement
**Primary route:** Economy → [Foreign Trade, WTO, FTAs and Protectionism](../../topics/economy/foreign-trade-wto-ftas-and-protectionism.md) → India-EU Broad-based Trade and Investment Agreement  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Foreign Trade, WTO, FTAs and Protectionism → India-EU Broad-based Trade and Investment Agreement  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q82 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/20_Foreign-Trade-WTO-FTAs-and-Protectionism.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/20_Foreign-Trade-WTO-FTAs-and-Protectionism.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-india-eu-broad-based-trade-and-investment-agreement`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

'Broad-based Trade and Investment Agreement (BTIA)' is sometimes seen in the news in the context of negotiations held between India and

(a) European Union  
(b) Gulf Cooperation Council  
(c) Organization for Economic Cooperation and Development  
(d) Shanghai Cooperation Organization  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — European Union**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The 'Broad-based Trade and Investment Agreement (BTIA)' refers to the comprehensive Free Trade Agreement (FTA) negotiations between India and the European Union (EU). These negotiations were officially launched on June 28, 2007, in Brussels, following a commitment made at the 7th India-EU Summit in Helsinki in 2006 (Ministry of Commerce and Industry). The agreement is designed to be 'broad-based' as it encompasses trade in goods, trade in services, and investment, alongside other areas such as intellectual property rights, government procurement, and competition policy. The primary objective is to enhance bilateral trade by removing barriers and providing a stable legal framework for investors (European Commission). Although negotiations were suspended in 2013 due to significant gaps in ambition regarding market access for automobiles, spirits, and the movement of professionals (Mode 4), they were formally relaunched in June 2022 during a meeting in Brussels (PIB).

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: European Union** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: Gulf Cooperation Council** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Organization for Economic Cooperation and Development** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q083"></a>
## Q83. WTO Trade Facilitation Agreement
**Primary route:** Economy → [Foreign Trade, WTO, FTAs and Protectionism](../../topics/economy/foreign-trade-wto-ftas-and-protectionism.md) → WTO Trade Facilitation Agreement  
**Primary classification:** Economy → Foreign Trade, WTO, FTAs and Protectionism → WTO Trade Facilitation Agreement  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q83 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/20_Foreign-Trade-WTO-FTAs-and-Protectionism.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/20_Foreign-Trade-WTO-FTAs-and-Protectionism.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-wto-trade-facilitation-agreement`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. India has ratified the Trade Facilitation Agreement (TFA) of WTO.
2. TFA is a part of WTO's Bali Ministerial Package of 2013.
3. TFA came into force in January 2016.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 1 and 3 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 and 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct: India ratified the WTO Trade Facilitation Agreement (TFA) on April 22, 2016. India was the 76th WTO member to accept the agreement, submitting its instrument of acceptance to the WTO Director-General. Statement 2 is correct: The TFA was a major outcome of the 9th Ministerial Conference held in Bali, Indonesia, in December 2013. It is considered the first multilateral agreement concluded since the establishment of the WTO in 1995 and was part of the broader 'Bali Ministerial Package'. Statement 3 is incorrect: The TFA did not come into force in January 2016. It officially entered into force on February 22, 2017, after the WTO reached the required threshold of ratification by two-thirds of its 164 members (110 members).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q084"></a>
## Q84. Strategic importance of Chabahar Port
**Primary route:** International Relations → [West Asia, Energy Security and Connectivity](../../topics/international-relations/west-asia-energy-security-and-connectivity.md) → Strategic importance of Chabahar Port  
**Primary classification:** International Relations → West Asia, Energy Security and Connectivity → Strategic importance of Chabahar Port  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q84 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [International-Relations/basic/06_West-Asia-Energy-Security-and-Connectivity.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/International-Relations/basic/06_West-Asia-Energy-Security-and-Connectivity.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `international-relations-strategic-importance-of-chabahar-port`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

What is the importance of developing Chabahar Port by India?

(a) India's trade with African countries will enormously increase.  
(b) India's relations with oil-producing Arab countries will be strengthened.  
(c) India will not depend on Pakistan for access to Afghanistan and Central Asia.  
(d) Pakistan will facilitate and protect the installation of a gas pipeline between Iraq and India.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — India will not depend on Pakistan for access to Afghanistan and Central Asia.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The development of the Chabahar Port in Iran is of immense strategic importance to India primarily because it provides a direct maritime-land access route to Afghanistan and the Central Asian Republics, effectively bypassing Pakistan. Historically, Pakistan has denied India land transit rights to reach Afghanistan, which has hindered India's trade and strategic connectivity with the region (PIB, 2016). The port, located in the Sistan-Baluchestan province on Iran's southern coast, allows Indian goods to reach the Iranian port and then move overland via the Zaranj-Delaram highway into Afghanistan (MEA, 2023). Furthermore, Chabahar is a critical component of the International North-South Transport Corridor (INSTC), which aims to connect India to Russia and Europe through Iran, significantly reducing transit time and costs compared to the traditional Suez Canal route (Drishti IAS, 2024). It also serves as a strategic counterweight to China's development of the Gwadar Port in Pakistan, located approximately 170 km away (The Hindu, 2024). Option 1 is incorrect because Iran is a Persian country, not an Arab country. Option 0 is incorrect as Africa is not the primary strategic focus of this specific port development. Option 3 is incorrect as Pakistan is not involved in facilitating such a pipeline via Chabahar.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: India's trade with African countries will enormously increase.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: India's relations with oil-producing Arab countries will be strengthened.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: India will not depend on Pakistan for access to Afghanistan and Central Asia.** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: Pakistan will facilitate and protect the installation of a gas pipeline between Iraq and India.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **strategic importance of Chabahar Port**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q085"></a>
## Q85. Mandatory cyber-security incident reporting
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Data Protection and Cybersecurity](../../topics/science-and-technology/data-protection-dpdp-act-and-cybersecurity.md) → Mandatory cyber-security incident reporting  
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Data Protection and Cybersecurity → Mandatory cyber-security incident reporting  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q85 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/12_Data-Protection-DPDP-Act-and-Cybersecurity.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/12_Data-Protection-DPDP-Act-and-Cybersecurity.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-mandatory-cyber-security-incident-reporting`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In India, it is legally mandatory for which of the following to report on cyber security incidents?
1. Service providers
2. Data centres
3. Body corporate

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 1 only  
(b) 1 and 2 only  
(c) 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

In India, the legal framework for reporting cyber security incidents is primarily governed by the Information Technology Act, 2000, and the rules framed thereunder. According to Section 70B of the IT Act, the Indian Computer Emergency Response Team (CERT-In) is the national nodal agency for incident response. Rule 12 of the 'Information Technology (The Indian Computer Emergency Response Team and Manner of Performing Functions and Duties) Rules, 2013' specifically mandates that 'service providers, intermediaries, data centres and body corporate' shall report cyber security incidents to CERT-In within a reasonable time of occurrence or noticing the incident. The term 'body corporate' is defined under Section 43A of the IT Act to include companies, firms, sole proprietorships, or other associations of individuals engaged in commercial or professional activities. Consequently, all three entities mentioned—service providers, data centres, and body corporate—are legally required to report such incidents. This mandate was further tightened by CERT-In directions issued on April 28, 2022, which introduced a strict 6-hour reporting window for specified types of incidents.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q086"></a>
## Q86. Right to vote and contest elections
**Primary route:** Polity → [Election Commission and Electoral Law](../../topics/polity/election-commission.md) → Right to vote and contest elections  
**Primary classification:** Polity → Election Commission and Electoral Law → Right to vote and contest elections  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q86 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Election-Commission.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Election-Commission.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-right-to-vote-and-contest-elections`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Right to vote and to be elected in India is a

(a) Fundamental Right  
(b) Natural Right  
(c) Constitutional Right  
(d) Legal Right  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Constitutional Right**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The 'Right to vote and to be elected' in India is considered a Constitutional Right. This is based on Article 326 of the Constitution of India, which stipulates that elections to the House of the People (Lok Sabha) and the Legislative Assemblies of States shall be on the basis of adult suffrage. While there has been significant judicial debate regarding whether this right is 'statutory' or 'constitutional'—with cases like 'Jyoti Basu v. Debi Ghoshal' (1982) and 'Kuldip Nayar v. Union of India' (2006) labeling it a statutory right—the Union Public Service Commission (UPSC) in its official answer key for the 2017 Civil Services Preliminary Examination (Question 86, Series A) explicitly identified 'Constitutional Right' as the correct answer. This interpretation is supported by the fact that the right originates directly from the constitutional text (Article 326) rather than solely from an Act of Parliament like the Representation of the People Act. Furthermore, the Supreme Court in 'PUCL v. Union of India' (2003) and 'Rajbala v. State of Haryana' (2015) has also characterized the right to vote as a constitutional right. NCERT Class 11 'Indian Constitution at Work' (Chapter 3) also discusses the right to vote within the framework of constitutional provisions.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Fundamental Right** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: Natural Right** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: Constitutional Right** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **D: Legal Right** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **right to vote and contest elections**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q087"></a>
## Q87. Evolved Laser Interferometer Space Antenna
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Space Programme and Gravitational-Wave Missions](../../topics/science-and-technology/space-programme-isro-launch-vehicles.md) → Evolved Laser Interferometer Space Antenna  
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Space Programme and Gravitational-Wave Missions → Evolved Laser Interferometer Space Antenna  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q87 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/01_Space-Programme-ISRO-Launch-Vehicles.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/01_Space-Programme-ISRO-Launch-Vehicles.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-evolved-laser-interferometer-space-antenna`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

What is the purpose of 'evolved Laser Interferometer Space Antenna (eLISA)' project?

(a) To detect neutrinos  
(b) To detect gravitational waves  
(c) To detect the effectiveness of missile defence system  
(d) To study the effect of solar flares on our communication systems  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — To detect gravitational waves**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The evolved Laser Interferometer Space Antenna (eLISA), now primarily known as LISA, is a space-based mission led by the European Space Agency (ESA) in collaboration with NASA. Its primary purpose is to detect and observe gravitational waves, which are ripples in the fabric of spacetime caused by massive cosmic events such as the merger of supermassive black holes or the orbits of compact binary stars (ESA, 2024). While ground-based observatories like LIGO (Laser Interferometer Gravitational-Wave Observatory) detect high-frequency gravitational waves, LISA is designed to detect low-frequency waves (0.1 mHz to 1 Hz) that are inaccessible from Earth due to seismic and gravity gradient noise (NASA, 2024). The mission involves three spacecraft flying in a triangular formation, separated by millions of kilometers, using laser interferometry to monitor their relative distances with extreme precision. The LISA Pathfinder mission, launched in 2015, successfully demonstrated the core technologies required for this project (PIB, 2016).

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: To detect neutrinos** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: To detect gravitational waves** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **C: To detect the effectiveness of missile defence system** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: To study the effect of solar flares on our communication systems** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **evolved Laser Interferometer Space Antenna**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

## Q88. Vidyanjali Yojana
**Primary route:** Social Justice → [Education and Human Resource Development](../../topics/social-justice/education-and-human-resource-development.md) → Vidyanjali Yojana  
**Primary classification:** Social Justice → Education and Human Resource Development → Vidyanjali Yojana

**Canonical owner:** [Social-Justice/basic/04_Education-and-Human-Resource-Development.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Social-Justice/basic/04_Education-and-Human-Resource-Development.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `social-justice-vidyanjali-yojana`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

What is the purpose of 'Vidyanjali Yojana'?
1. To enable the famous foreign educational institutions to open their campuses in India.
2. To increase the quality of education provided in government schools by taking help from the private sector and the community.
3. To encourage voluntary monetary contributions from private individuals and organizations so as to improve the infrastructure facilities for primary and secondary schools.

Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 2 only  
(b) 3 only  
(c) 1 and 2 only  
(d) 2 and 3 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The 'Vidyanjali Yojana' was launched by the Ministry of Human Resource Development (now Ministry of Education) in June 2016. Its primary objective is to enhance the quality of education in government schools by facilitating community and private sector involvement through volunteering. 

1. **Statement 1 is incorrect**: The scheme has no provision for enabling foreign educational institutions to open campuses in India. Such policies are typically covered under the Foreign Universities Bill or the National Education Policy (NEP) 2020.
2. **Statement 2 is correct**: The core purpose of Vidyanjali is to involve volunteers (such as retired professionals, NRIs, working professionals, and homemakers) to strengthen co-scholastic activities in government schools, thereby improving the overall quality of education through community support (PIB 2016).
3. **Statement 3 is incorrect**: The original version of the scheme (Vidyanjali 1.0, relevant to the 2017 question) specifically focused on the contribution of **services and skills** rather than monetary donations. The official guidelines at the time explicitly stated that the program was not for seeking financial contributions. While the revamped 'Vidyanjali 2.0' (launched in 2021) allows for the contribution of assets and materials, the 2017 UPSC question refers to the initial volunteer-service-based model.

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 2 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Primary route:** Social Justice → [Education and Human Resource Development](../../topics/social-justice/education-and-human-resource-development.md) → Unnat Bharat Abhiyan  
**Primary classification:** Social Justice → Education and Human Resource Development → Unnat Bharat Abhiyan

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

What is the aim of the programme 'Unnat Bharat Abhiyan'?

(a) Achieving 100% literacy by promoting collaboration between voluntary organizations and government's education system and local communities.  
(b) Connecting institutions of higher education with local communities to address development challenges through appropriate technologies.  
(c) Strengthening India's scientific research institutions in order to make India a scientific and technological power.  
(d) Developing human capital by allocating special funds for health care and education of rural and urban poor, and organizing skill development programmes and vocational training for them.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Connecting institutions of higher education with local communities to address development challenges through appropriate technologies.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Unnat Bharat Abhiyan (UBA) is a flagship program of the Ministry of Education (formerly Ministry of Human Resource Development), launched in 2014. Its primary objective is to connect Higher Education Institutions (HEIs) with local communities to address development challenges through appropriate technologies (unnatbharatabhiyan.gov.in). The program envisions transformational change in rural development processes by leveraging the knowledge base of institutions like IITs, NITs, and other HEIs to help build the architecture of an Inclusive India (PIB, 2018). Under this scheme, each participating institution adopts a cluster of at least five villages and works with the district administration and local communities to identify problems and provide technical solutions for sustainable growth and livelihood improvement (education.gov.in). IIT Delhi serves as the National Coordinating Institute for the program.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. The Election Commission of India is a five-member body.
2. Union Ministry of Home Affairs decides the election schedule for the conduct of both general elections and bye-elections.
3. Election Commission resolves the disputes relating to splits/mergers of recognised political parties.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 3 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect: The Election Commission of India (ECI) is currently a three-member body, consisting of the Chief Election Commissioner and two Election Commissioners. While the Constitution (Article 324) allows the President to fix the number of commissioners, it has functioned as a three-member body since October 1993 (M. Laxmikanth, Indian Polity, Chapter 42). Statement 2 is incorrect: The Election Commission of India is the authority that decides the election schedule for the conduct of both general elections and bye-elections under the powers of superintendence, direction, and control vested in it by Article 324 of the Constitution. The Ministry of Home Affairs does not decide the schedule. Statement 3 is correct: Under Paragraph 15 of the Election Symbols (Reservation and Allotment) Order, 1968, the Election Commission is the sole authority to decide on disputes arising out of splits or mergers of recognized political parties (ECI Official Website).

**Statement/item-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** Read with the reasoned explanation above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: 3 only** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **national park and river-location pairs**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.

<a id="2017-prelims-gs1-q091"></a>
## Q91. Schedule I protection under Wildlife Protection Act
**Primary route:** Environment and Ecology → [Wildlife Protection Act and Schedules](../../topics/environment/wildlife-protection-act-and-schedules.md) → Schedule I protection under Wildlife Protection Act  
**Primary classification:** Environment and Ecology → Wildlife Protection Act and Schedules → Schedule I protection under Wildlife Protection Act  
**Metadata:** 2017 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q91 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: factual/conceptual statement or option evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/08_Wildlife-Protection-Act-and-Schedules.md](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/08_Wildlife-Protection-Act-and-Schedules.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-and-ecology-schedule-i-protection-under-wildlife-protection-act`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In India, if a species of tortoise is declared protected under Schedule I of the Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972, what does it imply?

(a) It enjoys the same level of protection as the tiger.  
(b) It no longer exists in the wild, a few individuals are under captive protection; and now it is impossible to prevent its extinction.  
(c) It is endemic to a particular region of India.  
(d) Both (b) and (c) stated above are correct in this context.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — It enjoys the same level of protection as the tiger.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Under the Wildlife (Protection) Act (WPA), 1972, species listed in Schedule I are granted the highest level of legal protection in India. This implies that hunting these species is strictly prohibited across the country, except under extremely specific circumstances (such as being a threat to human life or being disabled beyond recovery), and only with the permission of the Chief Wildlife Warden. The Bengal Tiger is a prominent species listed in Schedule I; therefore, any tortoise species listed in the same schedule (such as the Indian Softshell Tortoise or the Hawksbill Turtle) enjoys the same rigorous protection and legal status as the tiger. Violations of the law concerning Schedule I species attract the harshest penalties, including mandatory imprisonment and heavy fines (Section 51 of the Act). Option 1 is incorrect because Schedule I is intended to protect species in the wild to prevent their extinction, not to declare them beyond saving. Option 2 is incorrect because Schedule I status is determined by the degree of threat and conservation priority, not exclusively by whether a species is endemic to a specific region.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: It enjoys the same level of protection as the tiger.** — Correct: this is the proposition/combination fixed by the UPSC official-final Series-A key and supported by the explanation.
- **B: It no longer exists in the wild, a few individuals are under captive protection; and now it is impossible to prevent its extinction.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **C: It is endemic to a particular region of India.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.
- **D: Both (b) and (c) stated above are correct in this context.** — Incorrect: it conflicts with at least one verified proposition or with the controlling official-final combination explained above.

**Elimination logic:** Identify the decisive distinction in **schedule I protection under Wildlife Protection Act**, reject options containing a demonstrably false statement or category mismatch, and then map the surviving set to A-D. Elimination corroborates evidence; it does not replace it.  
**UPSC trap:** The distractors exploit close institutional names, absolute qualifiers, chronology, category errors, or one partly true statement embedded in a wrong combination.
